## Introduction by the Author

# Introduction by the Author

Dear Reader,

As an educator and a lifelong admirer of English literature, I have always been passionate about helping students engage with classic works in meaningful ways. With this goal in mind, I am delighted to present "An Objective Approach to Mayor of Casterbridge: MCQs for RPSC Exams."

This book is the result of my years of experience teaching and studying Thomas Hardy's timeless novel, "The Mayor of Casterbridge." I have found that one of the most effective ways to deepen one's understanding of a literary work is through the practice of answering focused, thought-provoking questions. This is particularly true when preparing for exams like the RPSC First Grade Exam, where a strong grasp of the material is essential for success.

In creating this book, I have carefully crafted 47 sets of multiple-choice questions that cover a wide range of topics related to "The Mayor of Casterbridge." Each set is designed to test your knowledge of the novel's plot, characters, themes, and literary devices. By working through these questions, you will not only reinforce your understanding of the book but also identify areas where you may need to focus your study efforts.

My aim with this book is to provide you with a valuable resource that will help you approach your RPSC First Grade exam with confidence. Whether you are a student preparing for the RPSC First Grade Exam or simply a reader looking to engage with Hardy's work on a deeper level, I believe that "An Objective Approach to Mayor of Casterbridge" will be an invaluable companion on your journey.

I wish you all the best in your studies and in your future endeavors. Happy reading and learning!

Sincerely,

Mukesh K Sharma

## Set: 1

# Set: 1

Q1: What time period does the story take place in?

A) Early 17th century

B) Early 18th century

C) Early 19th century

D) Early 20th century

Answer: C

Q2: Where were the young man and woman approaching?

A) Lower Wessex

B) Weydon-Priors

C) Upper Sussex

D) Weatherbury

Answer: B

Q3: What was the man carrying on his back?

A) A knapsack

B) A rush basket

C) A leather satchel

D) A canvas bag

Answer: B

Q4: What was visible protruding from the man's basket?

A) A scythe

B) A hay-knife

C) A pitchfork

D) A sickle

Answer: B

Q5: What was peculiar about the couple's progress?

A) They walked very quickly

B) They frequently stopped to rest

C) They preserved perfect silence

D) They took a winding, indirect route

Answer: C

Q6: What was the chief attraction of the young woman's face?

A) Its mobility

B) Its paleness

C) Its perfect symmetry

D) Its delicate features

Answer: A

Q7: What was the little girl wearing on her feet?

A) Leather boots

B) Wooden clogs

C) Blue knitted yarn boots

D) Cloth slippers

Answer: C

Q8: What event was taking place in the village that day?

A) A harvest festival

B) A wedding

C) A fair

D) A funeral

Answer: C

Q9: What refreshment did the couple seek at the fair?

A) Roasted chestnuts

B) Meat pies

C) Furmity

D) Cider

Answer: C

Q10: Who presided over the furmity tent?

A) A young maiden

B) A jolly farmer

C) A haggish creature of about fifty

D) An elderly gentleman

Answer: C

Q11: What did the man secretly have added to his furmity?

A) Sugar

B) Rum

C) Cream

D) Spices

Answer: B

Q12: How did the man's demeanor change after drinking the laced furmity?

A) He became sleepy

B) He turned sullen and withdrawn

C) He grew overbearing and quarrelsome

D) He became overly affectionate

Answer: C

Q13: What was the theme of the conversation that arose?

A) The ruin of good men by bad wives

B) The joys of love and marriage

C) The importance of sobriety

D) The changing times

Answer: A

Q14: How much money did the man claim to have?

A) Less than five shillings

B) No more than ten shillings

C) Not more than fifteen shillings

D) At least twenty shillings

Answer: C

Q15: What shocking suggestion did the man make regarding wives?

A) That they should be cherished and adored

B) That they should manage the household finances

C) That they should be sold at auction

D) That they should work outside the home

Answer: C

Q16: Who praised the man's wife as having "true cultivation"?

A) A well-dressed gentleman

B) A smoking man who seemed a former servant

C) The furmity seller

D) One of the rustic locals

Answer: B

Q17: How much did the man initially offer to sell his wife for?

A) Five shillings

B) One guinea

C) Three guineas

D) Five guineas

Answer: D

Q18: Who unexpectedly agreed to buy the man's wife?

A) A wealthy farmer

B) The smoking gentleman

C) A sailor

D) The furmity seller

Answer: C

Q19: How much did the sailor pay for the wife?

A) Three guineas

B) Four pounds

C) Five guineas

D) Ten pounds

Answer: C

Q20: What did the woman do before leaving with the sailor?

A) Embraced her husband

B) Collected her belongings

C) Threw her wedding ring at her husband

D) Pleaded with her husband to reconsider

Answer: C

Q21: What did the woman take with her when she left?

A) A trunk of clothing

B) The furmity pot

C) Her child Elizabeth-Jane

D) The family dog

Answer: C

Q22: How did the husband react to his wife's departure?

A) With relief and joy

B) With a stolid look of concern

C) With anger and threats

D) With tears and pleas

Answer: B

Q23: What was notable about the horses outside the tent?

A) They were fighting amongst themselves

B) They were beautifully adorned with ribbons

C) They were waiting patiently to be harnessed

D) They were running loose without tethers

Answer: C

Q24: What did the husband vow regarding his wife?

A) That he would win her back

B) That he would seek revenge on the sailor

C) That he would not go after her

D) That he would leave town himself

Answer: C

Q25: Why did the furmity seller decide to let the man sleep in the tent?

A) She pitied his drunken state

B) He had paid her handsomely

C) The fair was continuing for several more days

D) She hoped he would buy more furmity in the morning

Answer: C

Q26: What color were the clouds in the west after sunset?

A) Rosy

B) Golden

C) Gray

D) Purple

Answer: A

Q27: How long had the woman been married to the man?

A) Six months

B) A year

C) A couple of years

D) Five years

Answer: C

Q28: What was the hay-trusser's trade?

A) Threshing wheat

B) Tending livestock

C) Cutting and bundling hay

D) Planting crops

Answer: C

Q29: What instrument was used to stir the furmity mixture?

A) A wooden spoon

B) A metal ladle

C) A large spoon

D) A long paddle

Answer: C

Q30: At what age did the man say he had married?

A) Sixteen

B) Eighteen

C) Twenty

D) Twenty-two

Answer: B

## Set: 2

# Set: 2

Q1: What was the man's name?

A) Michael Henchard

B) John Melvin

C) Thomas Briggs

D) William Dorset

Answer: A

Q2: What did the man see shining on the ground?

A) A gold coin

B) His wife's ring

C) A silver necklace

D) A metal button

Answer: B

Q3: What was the man carrying on his shoulders?

A) A sack of tools

B) A backpack

C) A basket of tools

D) A heavy bag

Answer: C

Q4: What kind of animal was the man's dog compared to?

A) A wolf

B) A fox

C) A cat

D) A bear

Answer: B

Q5: What was the man's occupation?

A) Farmer

B) Carpenter

C) Blacksmith

D) Hay-trusser

Answer: D

Q6: What was the man's wife's name?

A) Susan

B) Elizabeth

C) Mary

D) Jane

Answer: A

Q7: What did the man say he would do with his wife?

A) Divorce her

B) Sell her

C) Abandon her

D) Imprison her

Answer: B

Q8: Where did the man go after leaving the fair?

A) The village

B) The church

C) The pub

D) The market

Answer: B

Q9: What was the man's oath about?

A) Avoiding alcohol for 21 years

B) Being kind to his family

C) Finding his wife and child

D) Becoming a better person

Answer: A

Q10: What was the man's plan after making the oath?

A) Settle in Casterbridge

B) Return to the fair

C) Search for his wife and child

D) Find a new job

Answer: C

Q11: What did the man notice about the cottage's chimney?

A) It was emitting smoke

B) It was partially collapsed

C) It was unusually tall

D) It had no smoke coming from it

Answer: A

Q12: How did the man pay for his breakfast?

A) With coins

B) With the sailor's money

C) With a trifling payment

D) By doing chores for the housewife

Answer: C

Q13: How long did the man search for his wife and child?

A) A few days

B) A couple of weeks

C) Months

D) Years

Answer: C

Q14: What information did the man get at the seaport?

A) His wife and child had moved away

B) His wife and child had been found

C) His wife and child had emigrated

D) His wife and child were staying with the sailor

Answer: C

Q15: Where did the man decide to settle after his search?

A) Casterbridge

B) The village near the fair

C) The seaport town

D) His hometown

Answer: A

Q16: Where did the man wake up in the morning?

A) In his own bed

B) In a tent

C) On the ground

D) In a wagon

Answer: B

Q17: What was the man's initial reaction to realizing his wife was gone?

A) Happiness

B) Anger

C) Confusion

D) Sadness

Answer: C

Q18: What did the man say his wife's actions showed about her character?

A) She was reckless and resentful

B) She was meek and submissive

C) She was intelligent and cunning

D) She was foolish and simple

Answer: D

Q19: What did the man do after making his oath in the church?

A) He went to search for his wife

B) He went to find a job

C) He went to the village pub

D) He went to board a ship

Answer: A

Q20: What was unusual about the dogs the man saw at the fair?

A) They were aggressive and loud

B) They were well-trained and obedient

C) They were of mysterious breeds

D) They were small and timid

Answer: C

Q21: What time of day was it when the man left the fair?

A) Early morning

B) Midday

C) Evening

D) Late at night

Answer: A

Q22: What was the man's initial plan after realizing his wife was gone?

A) To go to the police

B) To get a job and save money

C) To get out of the situation as soon as possible

D) To return to the fair and search for her

Answer: C

Q23: What was the man's reaction to his wife's actions?

A) He was surprised and annoyed

B) He was understanding and forgiving

C) He was ashamed and embarrassed

D) He was relieved and indifferent

Answer: A

Q24: What was the man's reason for not following the investigation loudly?

A) He was afraid of the consequences

B) He was shy about revealing his conduct

C) He didn't have the resources to do so

D) He didn't care about finding his wife

Answer: B

Q25: What did the man notice about the village when he arrived?

A) It was bustling and active

B) It was quiet and still

C) It was deserted and abandoned

D) It was covered in a heavy fog

Answer: B

Q26: What was the significance of the man making his oath in the church?

A) It was a symbolic act of repentance

B) It was a necessary legal requirement

C) It was a personal tradition for him

D) It was a way to seek divine intervention

Answer: A

Q27: What was the man's final attitude towards his wife's actions?

A) He blamed her entirely

B) He took responsibility for them

C) He was indifferent to them

D) He didn't understand them

Answer: B

Q28: What was the man's primary motivation for searching for his wife and child?

A) To get his money back

B) To bring them home

C) To punish his wife

D) To clear his own name

Answer: B

Q29: What was the man's initial reaction to the events of the previous evening?

A) He was shocked and dismayed

B) He was amused and indifferent

C) He was curious and interested

D) He was relieved and content

Answer: A

Q30: What was the man's plan for settling in Casterbridge?

A) To start a new business

B) To find work as a laborer

C) To live a quiet, solitary life

D) To search for his family again

Answer: C

## Set: 3

# Set: 3

Q1: What happened to the hay-trusser's wife and child according to the text?

A) They accompanied him to the fair

B) They left him at the fair and disappeared

C) They died in an accident

D) Text doesn’t tell this

Answer: B

Q2: How much time had passed since the events of the previous chapter?

A) A few days

B) A couple of weeks

C) Months

D) Several years

Answer: D

Q3: What was the appearance of the widow who walked with her daughter?

A) She looked younger and more vibrant

B) She had lost some of her youthful appearance

C) She looked exactly the same as before

D) She was unrecognizable

Answer: B

Q4: How did the widow and her daughter appear to be related?

A) They were strangers

B) They were sisters

C) They were mother and daughter

D) They were aunt and niece

Answer: C

Q5: What did the daughter carry in her hand?

A) A basket

B) A bouquet of flowers

C) A book

D) A map

Answer: A

Q6: What had happened to the widow's companion, Richard Newson?

A) He was still alive

B) He had abandoned them

C) He had died at sea

D) He was in prison

Answer: C

Q7: What was the relationship between the widow and Michael Henchard?

A) They were siblings

B) They were spouses

C) They were distant relatives

D) They were acquaintances

Answer: C

Q8: How did the daughter feel about searching for Michael Henchard?

A) She was eager to find him

B) She was indifferent about it

C) She was opposed to the idea

D) She was curious but apprehensive

Answer: B

Q9: What did the old woman selling furmity remember about the hay-trusser?

A) She didn't remember him at all

B) She remembered him as a regular customer

C) She remembered him selling his wife

D) She remembered him being a troublemaker

Answer: C

Q10: What did the old woman say the hay-trusser had told her to do if anyone asked for him?

A) To say he had gone to Casterbridge

B) To say he had left the country

C) To say he had died

D) To say he couldn't be found

Answer: A

Q11: Why did the widow not reward the old woman more generously?

A) She didn't have the money

B) She was upset with the old woman

C) She didn't want to be seen associating with her

D) She didn't believe the old woman's story

Answer: C

Q12: Where did the widow and her daughter decide to go after leaving the fair?

A) Back home

B) To the village inn

C) To Casterbridge

D) To the police station

Answer: C

Q13: How did the daughter feel about the widow's interaction with the old woman?

A) She was proud of her mother

B) She was embarrassed by it

C) She was amused by it

D) She was indifferent to it

Answer: B

Q14: What had changed at the fair since the previous chapter?

A) It had grown significantly in size

B) It had become more modern and mechanized

C) It had become more crowded and lively

D) It had dwindled in scale and activity

Answer: D

Q15: What did the daughter say about the people at the fair changing "like the leaves of trees"?

A) She was impressed by the constant turnover

B) She was nostalgic for the way it used to be

C) She was frustrated by the lack of familiarity

D) She was indifferent to the changes

Answer: C

Q16: Why did the widow want to visit the fair again?

A) To reminisce about her past

B) To find her husband

C) To sell goods

D) To enjoy the atmosphere

Answer: A

Q17: What did the widow hope to find by visiting the fair?

A) Her lost husband

B) Old friends and acquaintances

C) Clues about her husband's whereabouts

D) A stall selling her favorite food

Answer: C

Q18: What was the old woman's occupation at the fair?

A) She ran a food stall

B) She worked as a fortune teller

C) She performed in a sideshow

D) She worked as a pickpocket

Answer: A

Q19: How did the old woman describe her former prosperous business?

A) She was proud of its success

B) She was nostalgic for the good old days

C) She was bitter about its decline

D) She was indifferent to its fate

Answer: B

Q20: What did the old woman offer to add to the widow's furmity?

A) Sugar

B) Spices

C) Cream

D) Rum

Answer: D

Q21: How did the widow react to the old woman's offer of added rum?

A) She accepted it gratefully

B) She declined politely

C) She became angry

D) She smiled bitterly

Answer: D

Q22: What did the widow learn from the old woman about her relative Michael Henchard?

A) That he had died

B) That he had moved to Casterbridge

C) That he had remarried

D) That he had left the country

Answer: B

Q23: Why did the daughter say it was "hardly respectable" for her mother to buy refreshments from the old woman?

A) She disapproved of the old woman's character

B) She didn't like the quality of the food

C) She was worried about the cost

D) She was concerned about her mother's reputation

Answer: D

Q24: How did the daughter appear to feel about searching for Michael Henchard?

A) She was eager to meet him

B) She was indifferent to the idea

C) She was wary and suspicious

D) She was excited by the prospect

Answer: B

Q25: How did the widow refer to herself when talking to the old woman?

A) Mrs. Henchard

B) Susan

C) Mrs. Newson

D) Mrs. Henchard-Newson

Answer: C

Q26: What did the widow say about the relationship between Michael Henchard and her daughter Elizabeth-Jane?

A) They were very close

B) They had never met

C) He was her father

D) He was a distant relative

Answer: B

Q27: Where did the widow and her daughter obtain lodging for the night?

A) In the fair itself

B) At an inn in the village

C) At the home of a relative

D) In a rented cottage

Answer: B

Q28: What did the daughter say about the respectability of the old woman's furmity stall?

A) It was a respectable business

B) It was beneath their dignity

C) It was a novelty attraction

D) It was a public nuisance

Answer: B

Q29: What did the widow say the old woman had told her about the hay-trusser's instructions?

A) To tell anyone who asked that he had died

B) To tell anyone who asked that he had moved away

C) To tell anyone who asked that he had gone to Casterbridge

D) To tell anyone who asked that she didn't know where he was

Answer: C

Q30: Why did the widow decide not to reward the old woman more generously?

A) She didn't have the money

B) She was angry with the old woman

C) She didn't believe the old woman's story

D) She didn't want to be seen associating with her

Answer: D

## Set: 5

# Set: 5

Q1: What difficulty did Susan Henchard face in telling her daughter the truth about her life?

A) She feared losing her daughter's strong affection

B) She felt a sense of wrong-doing on her own part

C) She believed her daughter was too young to understand

D) She thought the truth would not make a difference

Answer: A

Q2: What was Susan Henchard's simplistic belief about Newson's right to her?

A) He had acquired a morally real and justifiable right by his purchase

B) He had no real right to her as the transfer was not serious

C) He had a legal right to her but not a moral one

D) He had neither a legal nor a moral right to her

Answer: A

Q3: Where did Susan and Newson live for several years after leaving England?

A) Falmouth

B) Canada

C) Newfoundland

D) Casterbridge

Answer: B

Q4: What trade did Newson engage in after returning to England?

A) Fishing

B) Boatbuilding

C) Newfoundland trade

D) Farming

Answer: C

Q5: What event solved Susan's meek conscience about living with Newson?

A) Newson left home again for the Newfoundland trade

B) She received news of Newson's death at sea

C) A friend ridiculed her grave acceptance of her position

D) She decided to search for Henchard

Answer: B

Q6: How old was Elizabeth-Jane when she started to develop into womanliness?

A) Twelve

B) Fifteen

C) Eighteen

D) Twenty

Answer: C

Q7: What was Elizabeth-Jane's constant inquiry of her mother?

A) How to become more beautiful

B) How to find her real father

C) How to become a woman of wider knowledge and higher repute

D) How to escape poverty

Answer: C

Q8: What did Susan resolve to do without confiding in Elizabeth-Jane?

A) Search for her first husband, Henchard

B) Move to a new town to start over

C) Remarry to improve their circumstances

D) Put Elizabeth-Jane up for adoption

Answer: A

Q9: On what day of the week did Susan and Elizabeth-Jane reach the summit of a hill near Casterbridge?

A) Monday

B) Wednesday

C) Friday

D) Sunday

Answer: C

Q10: How did Elizabeth-Jane describe the appearance of Casterbridge from the hill?

A) A mosaic-work of subdued colors held together by a frame of deep green

B) An indistinct mass behind a dense stockade of limes and chestnuts

C) A huddled, old-fashioned place shut in by a square wall of trees

D) A modern town with many suburbs stretching into the countryside

Answer: C

Q11: What shape best characterized the antiquated borough of Casterbridge?

A) Circular

B) Triangular

C) Square

D) Hexagonal

Answer: C

Q12: How many main avenues ran from the center of each side of Casterbridge's tree-bound square?

A) One

B) Two

C) Three

D) Four

Answer: C

Q13: What did Susan think might have happened to Henchard when Elizabeth suggested running after the men who mentioned his name?

A) He might be in the workhouse or in the stocks

B) He might have left town to start a new life elsewhere

C) He might be a wealthy and respected man in town

D) He might not remember who they were anymore

Answer: A

Q14: What was unique about Casterbridge's dense avenue of trees?

A) It was a natural forest that had always been there

B) It was a new feature recently planted by the town council

C) It was actually the ancient defences of the town, planted as a promenade

D) It was a decorative feature with no historical significance

Answer: C

Q15: What conveyed a sense of great smugness and comfort inside Casterbridge?

A) The timber houses with overhanging stories

B) The lamplights glimmering through the engirdling trees

C) The sounds of a brass band playing

D) The agricultural and pastoral character of the shop window displays

Answer: B

Q16: What architectural feature was common in the houses of Casterbridge?

A) Brick-nogging that derived support from adjoining houses

B) Slate roofs patched with tiles and tile roofs patched with slate

C) Overhanging stories with small-paned lattices screened by dimity curtains

D) All of the above

Answer: D

Q17: What did the class of objects displayed in Casterbridge's shop windows indicate about the town's economy?

A) It was primarily industrial, with many factories

B) It relied heavily on agriculture and pastoral activities

C) It was a center for arts and cultural products

D) It specialized in luxury goods for wealthy patrons

Answer: B

Q18: What notable feature was observed about the church tower in Casterbridge?

A) It was newly constructed with modern materials

B) It leaned precariously to one side like the Tower of Pisa

C) Its stonework was heavily weathered, with mortar nibbled out of the joints

D) It was painted in bright colors to stand out against the sky

Answer: C

Q19: What was the purpose of the bell that began to toll after the clock struck eight?

A) To call people to evening church services

B) To signal the shutting of shops and the end of business for the day

C) To warn of an impending attack on the town

D) To announce the arrival of an important person

Answer: B

Q20: How did the shops respond to the tolling of the bell?

A) They immediately opened their doors to welcome evening customers

B) They ignored the bell and continued business as usual

C) They closed their shutters in a clatter through the whole length of the High Street

D) They turned off their lights but remained open for a while longer

Answer: C

Q21: What was unusual about the bread in Casterbridge at the time of Susan and Elizabeth-Jane's arrival?

A) It was moldy and inedible

B) It was exceptionally delicious and nutritious

C) It was flat and suet-like inside due to issues with the wheat used

D) It was scarce because the bakers were on strike

Answer: C

Q22: Who was responsible for the poor quality of the bread?

A) The millers who ground the wheat too finely

B) The bakers who used the wrong recipe

C) The corn-factor who sold the growers bad wheat

D) The farmers who harvested the wheat too early

Answer: C

Q23: What did Susan and Elizabeth-Jane do after learning about the bread situation?

A) They decided to leave Casterbridge immediately

B) They bought some of the bad bread to try it themselves

C) They got some biscuits as a temporary substitute for a meal

D) They confronted the corn-factor about his unethical practices

Answer: C

Q24: Where did Susan and Elizabeth-Jane instinctively go after getting the biscuits?

A) To the illuminated building where the brass band was playing

B) To the church to pray for better food

C) To the town hall to lodge a complaint about the bread

D) To the nearest inn to secure lodging for the night

Answer: A

Q25: What was Susan Henchard's motivation for not revealing her past to her daughter Elizabeth-Jane?

A) She wanted to protect her from the painful truth

B) She was ashamed of her own actions

C) She didn't think Elizabeth-Jane was mature enough to understand

D) She feared Elizabeth-Jane would judge her harshly

Answer: A

Q26: How did Susan's friend's ridicule impact her views on her situation with Newson?

A) It made her realize she could no longer live peacefully with Newson

B) It reinforced her belief that Newson had a rightful claim to her

C) It caused her to confront Newson about the immorality of their arrangement

D) It had no effect on her perspective or actions

Answer: A

Q27: What was the main factor preventing Elizabeth-Jane's mind from developing to its full potential?

A) Her mother's lack of education

B) The straitened circumstances of their poverty

C) Her own lack of motivation and curiosity

D) The limited opportunities available to women in her society

Answer: B

Q28: Why did Susan ultimately decide that searching for Henchard was the best course of action?

A) She wanted to reunite with her true love

B) She hoped he could provide financial support

C) She felt it was the proper thing to do as a free woman

D) She wanted to get revenge on him for selling her

Answer: C

Q29: What made Casterbridge feel so secure and content in the evening?

A) The lamplights glimmering through the trees that encircled the town

B) The sounds of the brass band playing cheerful music

C) The sight of agricultural tools displayed in the shop windows

D) The presence of the ancient Roman walls that still fortified the town

Answer: A

Q30: What prompted Casterbridge's inhabitants to close up their shops for the day?

A) The sounding of the curfew bell in the church tower

B) The sudden onset of a heavy rain shower

C) The arrival of the Mayor's procession through the streets

D) The flickering of the lamplights as they ran out of oil

Answer: A

10. How did Elizabeth-Jane describe the appearance of Casterbridge from the hill?
   A. A mosaic-work of subdued colors held together by a frame of deep green
   B. An indistinct mass behind a dense stockade of limes and chestnuts
   C. A huddled, old-fashioned place shut in by a square wall of trees
   D. A modern town with many suburbs stretching into the countryside
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

11. What shape best characterized the antiquated borough of Casterbridge?
   A. Circular
   B. Triangular
   C. Square
   D. Hexagonal
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

12. How many main avenues ran from the center of each side of Casterbridge's tree-bound square?
   A. One
   B. Two
   C. Three
   D. Four
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

13. What did Susan think might have happened to Henchard when Elizabeth suggested running after the men who mentioned his name?
   A. He might be in the workhouse or in the stocks
   B. He might have left town to start a new life elsewhere
   C. He might be a wealthy and respected man in town
   D. He might not remember who they were anymore
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

14. What was unique about Casterbridge's dense avenue of trees?
   A. It was a natural forest that had always been there
   B. It was a new feature recently planted by the town council
   C. It was actually the ancient defences of the town, planted as a promenade
   D. It was a decorative feature with no historical significance
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

15. What conveyed a sense of great smugness and comfort inside Casterbridge?
   A. The timber houses with overhanging stories
   B. The lamplights glimmering through the engirdling trees
   C. The sounds of a brass band playing
   D. The agricultural and pastoral character of the shop window displays
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

16. What architectural feature was common in the houses of Casterbridge?
   A. Brick-nogging that derived support from adjoining houses
   B. Slate roofs patched with tiles and tile roofs patched with slate
   C. Overhanging stories with small-paned lattices screened by dimity curtains
   D. All of the above
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

17. What did the class of objects displayed in Casterbridge's shop windows indicate about the town's economy?
   A. It was primarily industrial, with many factories
   B. It relied heavily on agriculture and pastoral activities
   C. It was a center for arts and cultural products
   D. It specialized in luxury goods for wealthy patrons
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

18. What notable feature was observed about the church tower in Casterbridge?
   A. It was newly constructed with modern materials
   B. It leaned precariously to one side like the Tower of Pisa
   C. Its stonework was heavily weathered, with mortar nibbled out of the joints
   D. It was painted in bright colors to stand out against the sky
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

19. What was the purpose of the bell that began to toll after the clock struck eight?
   A. To call people to evening church services
   B. To signal the shutting of shops and the end of business for the day
   C. To warn of an impending attack on the town
   D. To announce the arrival of an important person
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

20. How did the shops respond to the tolling of the bell?
   A. They immediately opened their doors to welcome evening customers
   B. They ignored the bell and continued business as usual
   C. They closed their shutters in a clatter through the whole length of the High Street
   D. They turned off their lights but remained open for a while longer
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

21. What was unusual about the bread in Casterbridge at the time of Susan and Elizabeth-Jane's arrival?
   A. It was moldy and inedible
   B. It was exceptionally delicious and nutritious
   C. It was flat and suet-like inside due to issues with the wheat used
   D. It was scarce because the bakers were on strike
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

22. Who was responsible for the poor quality of the bread?
   A. The millers who ground the wheat too finely
   B. The bakers who used the wrong recipe
   C. The corn-factor who sold the growers bad wheat
   D. The farmers who harvested the wheat too early
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

23. What did Susan and Elizabeth-Jane do after learning about the bread situation?
   A. They decided to leave Casterbridge immediately
   B. They bought some of the bad bread to try it themselves
   C. They got some biscuits as a temporary substitute for a meal
   D. They confronted the corn-factor about his unethical practices
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

24. Where did Susan and Elizabeth-Jane instinctively go after getting the biscuits?
   A. To the illuminated building where the brass band was playing
   B. To the church to pray for better food
   C. To the town hall to lodge a complaint about the bread
   D. To the nearest inn to secure lodging for the night
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

25. What was Susan Henchard's motivation for not revealing her past to her daughter Elizabeth-Jane?
   A. She wanted to protect her from the painful truth
   B. She was ashamed of her own actions
   C. She didn't think Elizabeth-Jane was mature enough to understand
   D. She feared Elizabeth-Jane would judge her harshly
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

26. How did Susan's friend's ridicule impact her views on her situation with Newson?
   A. It made her realize she could no longer live peacefully with Newson
   B. It reinforced her belief that Newson had a rightful claim to her
   C. It caused her to confront Newson about the immorality of their arrangement
   D. It had no effect on her perspective or actions
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

27. What was the main factor preventing Elizabeth-Jane's mind from developing to its full potential?
   A. Her mother's lack of education
   B. The straitened circumstances of their poverty
   C. Her own lack of motivation and curiosity
   D. The limited opportunities available to women in her society
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

28. Why did Susan ultimately decide that searching for Henchard was the best course of action?
   A. She wanted to reunite with her true love
   B. She hoped he could provide financial support
   C. She felt it was the proper thing to do as a free woman
   D. She wanted to get revenge on him for selling her
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

29. What made Casterbridge feel so secure and content in the evening?
   A. The lamplights glimmering through the trees that encircled the town
   B. The sounds of the brass band playing cheerful music
   C. The sight of agricultural tools displayed in the shop windows
   D. The presence of the ancient Roman walls that still fortified the town
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

30. What prompted Casterbridge's inhabitants to close up their shops for the day?
   A. The sounding of the curfew bell in the church tower
   B. The sudden onset of a heavy rain shower
   C. The arrival of the Mayor's procession through the streets
   D. The flickering of the lamplights as they ran out of oil
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

## Set: 6

# Set: 6

Q1: What was the main crop that Henchard dealt with?

A) Rice

B) Corn

C) Wheat

D) Soybeans

Answer: C

Q2: What was the cause of the poor quality of the bread made from Henchard's wheat?

A) The wheat was spoiled due to bad weather during the harvest.

B) The wheat was intentionally adulterated by Henchard.

C) The bakers did not know how to properly process the wheat.

D) There was a shortage of grain in the area.

Answer: A

Q3: What did Henchard say he would do to address the issue of the poor quality bread?

A) Offer to replace the spoiled flour with sound grain.

B) Admit fault and apologize to the customers.

C) Hire a manager to oversee the corn department.

D) Stop selling wheat and focus on other crops.

Answer: C

Q4: What was Henchard's occupation?

A) Farmer

B) Baker

C) Merchant

D) Mayor

Answer: D

Q5: Why did the crowd outside the hotel not feel the need to restrain their comments?

A) They were not invited to the dinner.

B) They were angry about the poor bread.

C) They were friends of Henchard.

D) They were trying to impress Elizabeth-Jane.

Answer: A

Q6: How did Henchard's appearance compare to when Elizabeth-Jane and her mother last saw him?

A) He was dressed more formally and had a higher social status.

B) He was dressed the same but had a more commanding presence.

C) He was dressed more casually and seemed less prosperous.

D) There was no change in his appearance or status.

Answer: A

Q7: What was the reason given for Henchard not drinking wine at the dinner?

A) He was recovering from an illness.

B) He was fasting for religious reasons.

C) He had taken a vow of abstinence.

D) He was trying to set an example for the other guests.

Answer: C

Q8: How long was Henchard's vow of abstinence supposed to last?

A) 1 year

B) 2 years

C) 5 years

D) Indefinitely

Answer: B

Q9: What was the reaction of the crowd outside the hotel to the discussion about the poor bread?

A) They were sympathetic to Henchard.

B) They were indifferent to the issue.

C) They found amusement in Henchard's discomfort.

D) They demanded that Henchard be removed from office.

Answer: C

Q10: What was the name of the hotel where the dinner was taking place?

A) The Crown

B) The Golden Lion

C) The King's Arms

D) The Town Hall

Answer: C

Q11: How long had Longways worked for Henchard?

A) 9 years

B) 19 years

C) 39 years

D) 69 years

Answer: D

Q12: What was the name of the dish that Henchard was eating when Elizabeth-Jane and her mother last saw him?

A) Furmity

B) Roast beef

C) Bread and cheese

D) Soup

Answer: A

Q13: What was the reason given for Henchard's rise to prominence in Casterbridge?

A) He inherited a successful business.

B) He married into a wealthy family.

C) He worked his way up from nothing.

D) He was born into a prominent family.

Answer: C

Q14: How did Henchard respond to the criticism about the poor bread?

A) He apologized and offered to make amends.

B) He dismissed the criticism as unimportant.

C) He blamed the weather and promised to hire a manager.

D) He threatened to take legal action against the bakers.

Answer: C

Q15: What was the name of the melody that the band was playing when the dinner ended?

A) "The Roast Beef of Old England"

B) "God Save the King"

C) "Auld Lang Syne"

D) The text does not specify the name of the melody.

Answer: D

Q16: What was Elizabeth-Jane's initial reaction to discovering that Henchard was her long-lost relative?

A) She was excited and eager to meet him.

B) She was skeptical and hesitant to approach him.

C) She was indifferent and did not see the significance.

D) She was intimidated and wanted to avoid him.

Answer: A

Q17: What was the reason given for Henchard's abstinence from alcohol?

A) He had a history of alcoholism.

B) It was part of a religious vow.

C) It was a personal choice to set an example.

D) It was a requirement of his position as Mayor.

Answer: B

Q18: What was the reaction of the crowd outside the hotel to Henchard's story about outwitting a sharper in a hay deal?

A) They were impressed and applauded his business acumen.

B) They were indifferent and did not engage with the story.

C) They found amusement in the story and laughed along.

D) They criticized Henchard for being dishonest in his dealings.

Answer: C

Q19: What was the social status of the group of tradesmen who interrupted Henchard's speech?

A) They were of a higher social status than the other guests.

B) They were of a lower social status than the other guests.

C) They were of the same social status as the other guests.

D) Their social status is not specified in the text.

Answer: B

Q20: What was the purpose of the dinner that Henchard was attending?

A) It was a political event for the town council.

B) It was a celebration of Henchard's success as a businessman.

C) It was a social gathering for the leading members of the community.

D) It was a fundraiser for a local charity.

Answer: C

Q21: What was the relationship between Henchard and Elizabeth-Jane's mother?

A) They were strangers to each other.

B) They were distant relatives.

C) They were close friends.

D) They were married.

Answer: D

Q22: How did Elizabeth-Jane's mother react to seeing Henchard at the dinner?

A) She was overjoyed and wanted to immediately approach him.

B) She was nervous and felt intimidated by his presence.

C) She was indifferent and did not seem to have any strong feelings.

D) She was angry and wanted to confront him about their past.

Answer: B

Q23: What was the reason given for Henchard's decision to advertise for a manager to oversee the corn department?

A) He wanted to expand his business and take on more contracts.

B) He was no longer able to manage the department effectively on his own.

C) He wanted to distance himself from the issues with the poor quality bread.

D) He was required to do so by the town council as a condition of his role as Mayor.

Answer: B

Q24: What was the reaction of the crowd outside the hotel to Henchard's response about the poor bread?

A) They were satisfied with his explanation and did not press the issue further.

B) They continued to criticize him and demand that he take responsibility.

C) They praised him for his honesty and openness in addressing the problem.

D) They remained silent and did not engage with Henchard's response.

Answer: B

Q25: How did Henchard's physical appearance and demeanor compare to the description of him from Elizabeth-Jane and her mother's previous encounter?

A) He seemed more refined and dignified.

B) He seemed more stern and unapproachable.

C) He seemed more relaxed and congenial.

D) There was no significant change in his appearance or demeanor.

Answer: A

Q26: What was the likely purpose of the old man's detailed description of Henchard's rise to prominence in Casterbridge?

A) To provide background information for Elizabeth-Jane.

B) To emphasize Henchard's importance and influence in the town.

C) To foreshadow potential conflicts or challenges Henchard may face.

D) To highlight the contrast between Henchard's current status and his humble beginnings.

Answer: D

Q27: What was the reason given for why the guests at the dinner were not filling Henchard's wine glasses?

A) They were respecting his choice to abstain from alcohol.

B) They were angry with him about the poor quality bread.

C) They were trying to limit his alcohol consumption for his own good.

D) They were saving the wine for the more important guests.

Answer: A

Q28: What was the name of the old man who provided Elizabeth-Jane with information about Henchard?

A) Christopher Coney

B) Solomon Longways

C) The text does not provide the old man's name.

D) The text refers to him only as "an old man".

Answer: B

Q29: What was the reaction of Elizabeth-Jane's mother to the discovery of Henchard's social standing and success?

A) She was impressed and wanted to approach him.

B) She was intimidated and wanted to avoid him.

C) She was indifferent and did not see the significance.

D) She was angry and wanted to confront him about their past.

Answer: B

Q30: What was the significance of the fact that Henchard was drinking water instead of wine at the dinner?

A) It was a sign of his wealth and status, as he could afford fine wines.

B) It was a reflection of his religious beliefs and dedication to abstinence.

C) It was an indication of his poor health or a medical condition.

D) It was a subtle way for him to set himself apart from the other guests.

Answer: B

   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

24. What was the purpose of Henchard's decision to "lower the dignity of his presence" and tone down his appearance before entering the Three Mariners?
   A. He wanted to avoid being recognized by the other patrons.
   B. He wanted to make a good impression on the young Scotchman.
   C. He wanted to maintain a sense of formality and authority.
   D. He wanted to blend in with the more ordinary clientele of the inn.
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

25. What was the significance of the fact that Henchard remained "stately and vertical" while the other guests became more disheveled and disorderly?
   A. It highlighted his superior social status and self-control.
   B. It foreshadowed his eventual downfall and loss of dignity.
   C. It demonstrated his discomfort and desire to distance himself from the event.
   D. It underscored his indifference and detachment from the proceedings.
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

26. What was the reason given for the young Scotchman's interest in the discussion about the poor bread?
   A. He was a baker or miller who had a professional interest in the topic.
   B. He was a social reformer who wanted to advocate for the rights of the poor.
   C. He was a traveler who was simply curious about the local issues being discussed.
   D. The text does not provide a clear explanation for his interest in the matter.
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

27. What was the significance of the fact that Henchard left the dinner to investigate the note, while the other guests remained engaged in their revelry?
   A. It showed Henchard's dedication to his duties as Mayor.
   B. It indicated that the note contained important information for Henchard.
   C. It highlighted Henchard's discomfort and desire to distance himself from the event.
   D. It foreshadowed Henchard's eventual separation from the social circle of the town.
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

## Set: 8

# Set: 8

Q1: Who are the two main characters that arrive at the Three Mariners inn?

A) Elizabeth-Jane and her mother

B) Henchard and Farfrae

C) Stannidge and his wife

D) Two unnamed travelers

Answer: A

Q2: Why does Elizabeth-Jane offer to help at the inn?

A) To earn extra money

B) To pay for their accommodation

C) Because she enjoys the work

D) At her mother's request

Answer: B

Q3: What does Elizabeth-Jane observe about the Scotchman's appearance?

A) His shining forehead and nicely cut hair

B) His muscular build and tan skin

C) His old-fashioned clothing and hat

D) His tired eyes and gray hair

Answer: A

Q4: What is the Mayor's name?

A) Joshua Jipp

B) Donald Farfrae

C) Henchard

D) Stannidge

Answer: C

Q5: Why does Henchard initially think Farfrae is there to see him?

A) In response to an advertisement for a corn-factor's manager

B) To sell him a new invention

C) To discuss a business partnership

D) To pay a social visit

Answer: A

Q6: Where is Farfrae actually planning to go?

A) Bristol

B) London

C) Scotland

D) America

Answer: D

Q7: What problem is Henchard facing with his wheat?

A) It is of poor quality

B) It has been stolen

C) It is infested with pests

D) It is overpriced

Answer: A

Q8: What does Farfrae claim his process can do to the bad wheat?

A) Make it taste better

B) Increase its nutritional value

C) Restore it to make good seconds

D) Make it last longer

Answer: C

Q9: What is Henchard's main business?

A) Corn and hay

B) Livestock

C) Baked goods

D) Textiles

Answer: A

Q10: What does Henchard offer Farfrae?

A) A job as his corn branch manager

B) A partnership in his business

C) A loan to start his own business

D) A place to stay in Casterbridge

Answer: A

Q11: Why does Farfrae decline Henchard's offer?

A) He doesn't think he's qualified

B) He doesn't like Henchard

C) He has plans to go to America

D) He wants to start his own business

Answer: C

Q12: What does Henchard say Farfrae's forehead reminds him of?

A) His own forehead

B) His father's forehead

C) His poor brother's forehead

D) His son's forehead

Answer: C

Q13: How tall is Henchard?

A) Five foot nine

B) Six foot one and a half

C) Five foot eleven

D) Six foot three

Answer: B

Q14: What does Henchard admit he is bad at?

A) Science and figures

B) Reading and writing

C) Communication and leadership

D) Farming and agriculture

Answer: A

Q15: What did Henchard do when he was a young man that he regrets?

A) Drank too much alcohol

B) Gambled away his money

C) Committed a crime

D) Married the wrong woman

Answer: A

Q16: What did Henchard swear to do after his youthful mistake?

A) Never drink alcohol again

B) Only drink tea

C) Never gamble again

D) Dedicate himself to business

Answer: B

Q17: Despite his oath, what does Henchard say he sometimes feels like doing on hot days?

A) Drinking a quarter-barrel of beer

B) Gambling at the horse races

C) Swimming in the river

D) Eating a whole pie

Answer: A

Q18: How does Farfrae react to Henchard's conviction about his value?

A) He is moved by it

B) He is skeptical of it

C) He is offended by it

D) He ignores it

Answer: A

Q19: What is the main reason Farfrae gives for declining Henchard's offer?

A) He wants to see the world

B) He doesn't think he would be a good manager

C) He doesn't want to live in Casterbridge

D) He has a better offer elsewhere

Answer: A

Q20: What kind of ale does Farfrae find warming to the stomach?

A) American ale

B) Bristol ale

C) Scottish ale

D) Casterbridge ale

Answer: D

Q21: How do Elizabeth-Jane and her mother react to overhearing the conversation?

A) They are surprised by the revelations

B) They are angry at Henchard

C) They feel sorry for Farfrae

D) They are indifferent to it

Answer: A

Q22: What is the relationship between Elizabeth-Jane's mother and Henchard?

A) They are simple kin

B) They are old friends

C) They are former lovers

D) It is not explicitly stated

Answer: D

Q23: What does Henchard say keeps a business established?

A) Judgment and knowledge

B) Strength and bustle

C) Hard work and dedication

D) Luck and timing

Answer: A

Q24: What does Farfrae say he has that could be useful to the corn trade?

A) Some inventions

B) Years of experience

C) Important connections

D) Significant financial resources

Answer: A

Q25: How does Henchard try to persuade Farfrae to stay and work for him?

A) By offering to pay him well

B) By appealing to his sense of loyalty

C) By threatening to blackmail him

D) By promising him a partnership

Answer: A

Q26: What does Henchard say about Farfrae's nose?

A) It is similar to his own nose

B) It is not unlike his brother's nose

C) It is the most distinctive feature on his face

D) It reminds him of someone he used to know

Answer: B

Q27: How does Farfrae address Henchard throughout their conversation?

A) By his first name

B) As "Mister"

C) As "Sir"

D) Using his title of "Mayor"

Answer: C

Q28: What does Henchard say he will be ashamed of until his dying day?

A) A deed he did on account of drinking too much

B) Failing to keep his business successful

C) Not being able to persuade Farfrae to stay

D) His lack of education and refinement

Answer: A

Q29: What does Farfrae say about Henchard's offer to drink with him?

A) He accepts eagerly

B) He declines politely

C) He says he will consider it

D) He suggests drinking tea instead

Answer: B

Q30: What is Farfrae's main focus and priority?

A) Gaining wealth and success

B) Advancing his career in the corn trade

C) Settling down and starting a family

D) Traveling and seeing the world

Answer: D

## Set: 9

# Set: 9

Q1: What was the name of the young Scotchman who sang at the Three Mariners?

A) Donald Farfrae

B) Christopher Coney

C) Solomon Longways

D) Buzzford

Answer: A

Q2: Where was the young Scotchman heading after his stay in Casterbridge?

A) London

B) Edinburgh

C) Bristol

D) Glasgow

Answer: C

Q3: What was Elizabeth-Jane's reaction to the young Scotchman's singing?

A) She was indifferent to it.

B) She was annoyed by it.

C) She was enraptured by it.

D) She was embarrassed by it.

Answer: C

Q4: Who did Elizabeth-Jane's mother think the young Scotchman might befriend and take up?

A) Elizabeth-Jane

B) Henchard

C) Donald Farfrae

D) Coney

Answer: A

Q5: What did the young Scotchman say about his country that corrected the misconceptions of the Casterbridge residents?

A) It has perpetual snow and wolves.

B) It has beautiful scenery in the summer.

C) It is a land of gaberlunzies.

D) It is a barren and ignorant place.

Answer: B

Q6: How did the young Scotchman respond to the Casterbridge residents' grim and broad way of talking about themselves?

A) He was amused by it.

B) He was offended by it.

C) He was indifferent to it.

D) He was saddened by it.

Answer: D

Q7: What did the young Scotchman say was the reason he was leaving Casterbridge?

A) He was homesick.

B) He had a job opportunity elsewhere.

C) He was on his way to foreign parts.

D) He disliked the Casterbridge residents.

Answer: C

Q8: How did Henchard feel about the young Scotchman's singing voice?

A) He was annoyed by it.

B) He was indifferent to it.

C) He was captivated by it.

D) He was offended by it.

Answer: C

Q9: What did Elizabeth-Jane's mother express concern about regarding the young Scotchman befriending them?

A) It would damage his reputation as Mayor.

B) It would make him uncomfortable.

C) It would cause him financial trouble.

D) It would make him homesick.

Answer: A

Q10: How did Elizabeth-Jane feel about serving the young Scotchman at the inn?

A) She was nervous about it.

B) She was eager to do it.

C) She was indifferent to it.

D) She was embarrassed by it.

Answer: B

Q11: What was the reason Elizabeth-Jane gave for her mother's concern about the young Scotchman befriending them?

A) It would grieve his natural pride as Mayor.

B) It would damage his reputation in the town.

C) It would make him uncomfortable with their social status.

D) It would cause him financial trouble.

Answer: A

Q12: What did Elizabeth-Jane admire about the young Scotchman's perspective on life and its surroundings?

A) His serious and thoughtful nature

B) His ability to find humor in the grim realities

C) His appreciation for the beauty of his homeland

D) His indifference to the opinions of the Casterbridge residents

Answer: A

Q13: When Elizabeth-Jane encountered the young Scotchman on the stairs, how did he respond to her?

A) He ignored her.

B) He greeted her politely.

C) He sang a song to her.

D) He asked her questions about herself.

Answer: C

Q14: What did the young Scotchman say was the reason he was undertaking the voyage away from his homeland?

A) He was seeking a better life.

B) He was running away from his problems.

C) He was following a job opportunity.

D) He was determined to achieve the prizes of life.

Answer: D

Q15: How did the Casterbridge residents view the young Scotchman's singing as compared to their own local songs?

A) They found it superior and deeply moving.

B) They found it overly sentimental and simplistic.

C) They found it amusing and entertaining.

D) They found it irrelevant and out of place.

Answer: A

Q16: What did the young Scotchman say was the reason for the perpetual snow and wolves in his homeland?

A) It was a barren and ignorant place.

B) It was a land of gaberlunzies.

C) It was simply a misconception.

D) It was a tragic rather than comical reality.

Answer: C

Q17: How did Elizabeth-Jane's mother feel about her serving the young Scotchman at the inn?

A) She was proud of Elizabeth-Jane's willingness to serve.

B) She was concerned it would damage the young Scotchman's opinion of them.

C) She was indifferent, as it was just a temporary situation.

D) She was embarrassed that her daughter had to serve at an inn.

Answer: B

Q18: What did the young Scotchman say he had sent ahead of himself for the voyage?

A) His flock bed and quilt.

B) His luggage, though it wasn't much.

C) His crock and bit of chiney.

D) He was traveling with bare bones.

Answer: B

Q19: How did the Casterbridge residents react to the young Scotchman's singing of "Auld Lang Syne"?

A) They were unimpressed and continued their conversations.

B) They were moved to tears by the emotion in his performance.

C) They burst into applause and demanded an encore.

D) They were amused by his choice of an old Scottish song.

Answer: C

Q20: What did the young Scotchman say was the reason he had decided to go on the voyage?

A) He was homesick for his homeland.

B) He was seeking a better life abroad.

C) He was following a job opportunity.

D) He was determined to achieve the prizes of life.

Answer: D

Q21: How did Elizabeth-Jane's mother feel about the young Scotchman potentially befriending them?

A) She was hopeful it would improve their situation.

B) She was worried it would damage his reputation.

C) She was indifferent, as she didn't think it would happen.

D) She was concerned it would make him uncomfortable.

Answer: B

Q22: What did the young Scotchman say about the scenery in his homeland that corrected the Casterbridge residents' misconceptions?

A) It had perpetual snow and dangerous animals.

B) It was a barren and ignorant place.

C) It had beautiful scenery in the summer months.

D) It was a land of gaberlunzies and tragic realities.

Answer: C

Q23: How did Elizabeth-Jane feel about the young Scotchman's perspective on life and its surroundings?

A) She found it overly serious and gloomy.

B) She found it fascinating and similar to her own.

C) She found it amusing and out of touch with reality.

# Set: 10

Q1: What was the first indication Elizabeth-Jane noticed that autumn was approaching Casterbridge?

A) The appearance of thistledown floating in the air

B) The sound of the wind rustling the leaves

C) The sight of farmers bringing in the hay harvest

D) The smell of ripening crops in the fields

Answer: A

Q2: Where did Henchard and Farfrae go after leaving the Three Mariners?

A) They went to the King's Arms Hotel

B) They went to the Market House

C) They went up the Bristol Road

D) They went to Henchard's offices

Answer: C

Q3: How did Elizabeth-Jane feel about Farfrae not acknowledging her when he left the inn?

A) She was indifferent to it

B) She was offended by his rudeness

C) She felt a latent sense of slight

D) She was curious about his behavior

Answer: C

Q4: What did Elizabeth-Jane's mother hope would happen if Henchard recognized them as his kin?

A) That he would take them in and support them

B) That he would be pleased to see them again

C) That he would help Elizabeth-Jane find a job

D) That he would at least be cordial to them

Answer: A

Q5: What did Elizabeth-Jane notice about the fronts of the houses in Casterbridge compared to the backs?

A) The fronts were more modern and well-maintained

B) The fronts were more ornate and decorative

C) The fronts were older and more traditional

D) The fronts were less inviting and open

Answer: C

Q6: What was the purpose of Elizabeth-Jane's visit to Henchard's offices?

B) To apply for a job with Henchard

C) To inquire about buying grain from Henchard

D) To confront Henchard about his past behavior

Answer: A

B) They were neatly organized and well-tended

C) They were being actively packed and loaded

D) They were untouched, waiting for delivery

Answer: C

Q8: What did Henchard say was the reason he wanted Farfrae to stay and work for him?

D) All of the above

Answer: D

Answer: C

D) All of the above

   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

6. What was the purpose of Elizabeth-Jane's visit to Henchard's offices?
   A. To deliver a message from her mother
   B. To apply for a job with Henchard
   C. To inquire about buying grain from Henchard
   D. To confront Henchard about his past behavior
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

7. What did Elizabeth-Jane notice about the state of the sample-bags and wheat in Henchard's yard?
   A. They were in disarray and neglected
   B. They were neatly organized and well-tended
   C. They were being actively packed and loaded
   D. They were untouched, waiting for delivery
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

8. What did Henchard say was the reason he wanted Farfrae to stay and work for him?
   A. Farfrae's intellect and skills were necessary for the business
   B. Henchard genuinely liked Farfrae and wanted his company
   C. Henchard was lonely and needed someone to talk to
   D. All of the above
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

9. What did Farfrae say was the reason he had decided to stay and work for Henchard?
   A. He was persuaded by Henchard's offer
   B. He felt it was the will of Providence
   C. He wanted to stay in Casterbridge
   D. All of the above
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

10. Where did Henchard take Farfrae after they made their agreement?
   A. To the Three Mariners inn
   B. To Henchard's residence
   C. To the town's market square
   D. To Henchard's offices and stores
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

11. How did Henchard describe his feelings towards Farfrae before they reached their agreement?
   A. He was indifferent to Farfrae
   B. He disliked Farfrae and wanted him to leave
   C. He felt a strong liking and fondness for Farfrae
   D. He was suspicious and distrustful of Farfrae
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

12. What did Henchard offer Farfrae as they finalized their agreement?
   A. A share in the business
   B. A room in Henchard's house
   C. A generous salary and benefits
   D. All of the above
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

13. What did Henchard say was the reason he was so eager for Farfrae to stay and work for him?
   A. Farfrae's skills were indispensable for the business
   B. Henchard was lonely and wanted Farfrae's company
   C. Henchard felt a strong personal liking and fondness for Farfrae
   D. All of the above
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

14. How did Farfrae react when Henchard expressed his desire for Farfrae to stay?
   A. He was reluctant and hesitant
   B. He was enthusiastic and agreeable
   C. He was suspicious of Henchard's motives
   D. He was indifferent to Henchard's offer
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

15. What indication was given that Henchard and Farfrae had reached a formal agreement?
   A. They shook hands on the deal
   B. They signed a written contract
   C. Farfrae agreed to cancel his travel plans
   D. Henchard offered Farfrae lodging in his home
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

16. Where did Henchard and Farfrae go after finalizing their agreement?
   A. To the Three Mariners inn
   B. To Henchard's offices and stores
   C. To Henchard's residence
   D. To the town's market square
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

17. What did Henchard promise Farfrae he would do regarding Farfrae's luggage?
   A. He would have it sent to Henchard's house
   B. He would have it stored at Henchard's offices
   C. He would arrange for it to be forwarded to Bristol
   D. He would have it delivered to wherever Farfrae was staying
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

18. What did Elizabeth-Jane notice about the way the Casterbridge residents communicated without words?
   A. They used exaggerated facial expressions and body language
   B. They spoke in a regional dialect that was difficult to understand
   C. They maintained a formal and reserved demeanor in public
   D. They relied on written notes and letters to convey information
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

19. What did Elizabeth-Jane notice about the layout and design of the houses in Casterbridge?
   A. They were modern and uniform in appearance
   B. They had elaborate and ornate architectural features
   C. They had a mix of old and new construction styles
   D. They were arranged in a haphazard and crowded manner
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

20. What was the significance of the fact that Henchard's house was one of the best in Casterbridge?
   A. It indicated his wealth and status in the community
   B. It suggested he had a welcoming and hospitable nature
   C. It made it an appealing destination for Elizabeth-Jane's visit
   D. All of the above
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

21. What did Elizabeth-Jane notice about the activity and clutter in the streets of Casterbridge on market day?
   A. The streets were empty and quiet
   B. There were many carts and stalls obstructing passage
   C. The residents were engaging in lively conversations
   D. The constables were strictly enforcing traffic regulations
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

22. What did Elizabeth-Jane notice about the relationship between the Casterbridge townspeople and the surrounding rural community?
   A. They were isolated and disconnected from each other
   B. They had little understanding or concern for one another
   C. They were closely intertwined and interdependent
   D. They viewed each other with suspicion and resentment
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

23. What was Elizabeth-Jane's initial reaction when she encountered Farfrae in Henchard's offices instead of Henchard himself?
   A. She was pleased to see Farfrae again
   B. She was confused and uncertain
   C. She was disappointed not to find Henchard
   D. She was annoyed at the unexpected change
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

24. What did Henchard say were the reasons he wanted Farfrae to stay and work for him?
   A. Farfrae's skills and intellect were necessary for the business
   B. Henchard genuinely liked Farfrae and wanted his company
   C. Henchard was lonely and needed someone to talk to
   D. All of the above
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

25. How did Farfrae describe his original plans before Henchard persuaded him to stay?
   A. He was traveling to America
   B. He was going to Bristol
   C. His plans were vague and uncertain
   D. He was returning to his homeland in Scotland
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

26. What did Henchard promise Farfrae in order to convince him to stay?
   A. A share in the business and a generous salary
   B. Room and board in Henchard's own residence
   C. No specific terms, just that Farfrae could name his own
   D. All of the above
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

27. How did Henchard describe his feelings towards Farfrae before they reached their agreement?
   A. He was indifferent and disinterested in Farfrae
   B. He actively disliked Farfrae and wanted him to leave
   C. He felt a strong personal liking and fondness for Farfrae
   D. He was suspicious and distrustful of Farfrae's motives
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

28. What was the significance of Henchard taking Farfrae to see his offices, stores, and stock?
   A. He wanted to demonstrate the scale and sophistication of his business
   B. He was proud to show off his wealth and success to Farfrae
   C. He was ensuring Farfrae fully understood the scope of his responsibilities
   D. All of the above
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

29. How did Henchard describe his tendency to either greatly dislike or greatly like certain people?
   A. He said he was distant and indifferent to those he didn't care for
   B. He said he would be harsh and critical towards those he disliked
   C. He said he would be fiercely loyal and devoted to those he liked
   D. All of the above
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

30. What did Henchard offer Farfrae in terms of living arrangements after their agreement?
   A. He invited Farfrae to stay in his own home
   B. He said he would help Farfrae find suitable lodgings
   C. He said Farfrae could stay in the offices or storerooms
   D. He left the living arrangements up to Farfrae to decide
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

## Set: 11

# Set: 11

Q1: What was the newcomer's name?

A) Joshua Jopp

B) Donald Farfrae

C) Susan Newson

D) Elizabeth-Jane

Answer: A

Q2: What position did Joshua Jopp claim to have?

A) Accountant

B) Salesman

C) New manager

D) Clerk

Answer: C

Q3: What did Henchard tell Joshua Jopp about the new manager position?

A) He had already hired someone else.

B) The position was no longer available.

C) Jopp had to come back on Thursday.

D) Jopp needed more experience.

Answer: A

Q4: What was the relationship between Elizabeth-Jane and Susan Newson?

A) Sisters

B) Mother and daughter

C) Cousins

D) Strangers

Answer: B

Q5: How did Henchard react when he learned that Susan Newson was still alive?

A) He was overjoyed.

B) He was indifferent.

C) He was surprised.

D) He was angry.

Answer: C

Q6: What did Henchard write in the note he gave to Elizabeth-Jane?

A) He invited Susan Newson to dinner.

B) He asked Elizabeth-Jane to come visit him.

C) He asked to meet with Susan Newson that evening.

D) He sent money to help Susan Newson.

Answer: C

Q7: Why did Henchard seem concerned that Susan and Elizabeth-Jane might be "impostors"?

A) He thought they were trying to trick him.

B) He didn't believe they were really related to him.

C) He was worried they were pretending to be someone else.

D) He was unsure if his wife and daughter were actually dead.

Answer: D

Q8: What was Elizabeth-Jane's reaction when Henchard held her hand at parting?

A) She was indifferent.

B) She was annoyed.

C) She was much affected and had tears in her eyes.

D) She was surprised.

Answer: C

Q9: Where did Henchard and Elizabeth-Jane have their conversation?

A) In Henchard's office

B) In the dining room of Henchard's house

C) At the Three Mariners Inn

D) In the garden

Answer: B

Q10: What did Henchard say about Elizabeth-Jane's mother being "not left very well off"?

A) He offered to help her financially.

B) He expressed sympathy for her situation.

C) He was upset that she was poor.

D) He thought she was pretending to be poor.

Answer: B

Q11: How did Elizabeth-Jane describe her and her mother's journey to Casterbridge?

A) They had been in England some years after coming from Canada.

B) They had just arrived from America or Australia.

C) They had been traveling for a long time.

D) They had always lived in Casterbridge.

Answer: A

Q12: What was the nature of Henchard's relationship with Elizabeth-Jane's mother, Susan Newson, in the past?

A) They were close friends.

B) They were distant relatives.

C) They were married.

D) They had no previous relationship.

Answer: C

Q13: What did Henchard say about the "transaction of his early married life" with Susan Newson?

A) It was well-known in their family.

B) It was a source of embarrassment.

C) It was a secret that had never been revealed.

D) It was a happy memory for him.

Answer: C

Q14: How did Henchard describe Elizabeth-Jane's clothing?

A) As fashionable and stylish.

B) As old-fashioned and worn.

C) As tattered and poor.

D) As formal and proper.

Answer: B

Q15: What did Henchard enclose with the note he gave to Elizabeth-Jane?

A) A letter to Susan Newson.

B) A request for a meeting.

C) A five-pound note.

D) An invitation to dinner.

Answer: C

Q16: Why did Henchard seem suddenly "much moved" when he received the note from Elizabeth-Jane?

A) He was angry about the contents.

B) He was excited to see his daughter again.

C) He was confused by the news it contained.

D) He was worried about what it might reveal.

Answer: C

Q17: What did Susan Newson do when she received the note from Henchard?

A) She read it immediately.

B) She asked Elizabeth-Jane about her meeting with Henchard.

C) She told Elizabeth-Jane she was going to meet Henchard alone.

D) All of the above.

Answer: D

Q18: Where did Henchard arrange to meet Susan Newson in the note?

A) At his house

B) At the Three Mariners Inn

C) At the Ring on the Budmouth road

D) He did not specify a location.

Answer: C

Q19: How did Henchard describe Elizabeth-Jane when she first entered his office?

A) As a "young woman"

B) As a "child"

C) As his "relative"

D) As "Elizabeth-Jane Newson"

Answer: A

Q20: What was Donald Farfrae doing in Henchard's office when Elizabeth-Jane arrived?

A) Waiting to speak with Henchard

B) Overseeing the financial records

C) Overhauling bins and samples

D) Handling correspondence

Answer: C

Q21: What detail is mentioned about the furnishings in Henchard's dining room?

A) The room had a fireplace with a fluted semi-circular back.

B) The table had low-hanging leaves that nearly touched the floor.

C) The room contained a family Bible, a "Josephus", and a "Whole Duty of Man".

D) All of the above.

Answer: D

Q22: What was the relationship between Henchard and the newcomer, Joshua Jopp?

A) They were business partners.

B) They were strangers.

C) They were longtime friends.

D) They had a previous employment arrangement.

Answer: D

Q23: How did Henchard respond when Elizabeth-Jane mentioned that her father had died at sea?

A) He was sympathetic.

B) He was indifferent.

C) He was visibly upset.

D) He asked for more details.

Answer: C

Q24: What did Henchard say about Elizabeth-Jane's mother being "rather worn out, with travelling"?

A) He offered to help her rest.

B) He was concerned for her well-being.

C) He thought she was making excuses.

D) He questioned the truthfulness of the statement.

Answer: B

Q25: How did Henchard behave towards Elizabeth-Jane when showing her into his home?

A) He was brusque and impatient.

B) He was formal and distant.

C) He was gentle and surprisingly delicate.

D) He was eager and overly familiar.

Answer: C

Q26: What did Henchard do after Elizabeth-Jane left the room?

A) He sat stiffly, gazing at the wall.

B) He immediately left to go meet Susan Newson.

C) He began to write another letter.

D) He resumed his work with Donald Farfrae.

Answer: A

Q27: What did Henchard mean when he said "It never rains but it pours!"?

A) He was happy about the sudden turn of events.

B) He was overwhelmed by the unexpected news.

C) He was frustrated by the interruption to his work.

D) He was concerned about potential problems.

Answer: B

Q28: Why did Susan Newson not read Henchard's note immediately?

A) She was busy with other tasks.

B) She wanted Elizabeth-Jane to describe the meeting first.

C) She was suspicious of the contents.

D) She wanted to keep the contents a secret from Elizabeth-Jane.

Answer: D

Q29: What did the amount of five guineas enclosed in Henchard's note likely signify?

A) It was a generous gift to help Susan Newson.

B) It was payment for some past debt or obligation.

C) It was a bribe to keep Elizabeth-Jane unaware of the situation.

D) It was intended as recompense for some wrongdoing.

Answer: D

Q30: How did the text describe Henchard's physical appearance, particularly his eyes?

A) His eyes had a piercing, intense gaze.

B) His eyes had a red spark of light in them.

C) His eyes were large and expressive.

D) His eyes were dull and lifeless.

Answer: B
