   D. All of the above.
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

18. Where did Henchard arrange to meet Susan Newson in the note?
   A. At his house
   B. At the Three Mariners Inn
   C. At the Ring on the Budmouth road
   D. He did not specify a location.
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

19. How did Henchard describe Elizabeth-Jane when she first entered his office?
   A. As a "young woman"
   B. As a "child"
   C. As his "relative"
   D. As "Elizabeth-Jane Newson"
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

20. What was Donald Farfrae doing in Henchard's office when Elizabeth-Jane arrived?
   A. Waiting to speak with Henchard
   B. Overseeing the financial records
   C. Overhauling bins and samples
   D. Handling correspondence
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

21. What detail is mentioned about the furnishings in Henchard's dining room?
   A. The room had a fireplace with a fluted semi-circular back.
   B. The table had low-hanging leaves that nearly touched the floor.
   C. The room contained a family Bible, a "Josephus", and a "Whole Duty of Man".
   D. All of the above.
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

22. What was the relationship between Henchard and the newcomer, Joshua Jopp?
   A. They were business partners.
   B. They were strangers.
   C. They were longtime friends.
   D. They had a previous employment arrangement.
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

23. How did Henchard respond when Elizabeth-Jane mentioned that her father had died at sea?
   A. He was sympathetic.
   B. He was indifferent.
   C. He was visibly upset.
   D. He asked for more details.
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

24. What did Henchard say about Elizabeth-Jane's mother being "rather worn out, with travelling"?
   A. He offered to help her rest.
   B. He was concerned for her well-being.
   C. He thought she was making excuses.
   D. He questioned the truthfulness of the statement.
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

25. How did Henchard behave towards Elizabeth-Jane when showing her into his home?
   A. He was brusque and impatient.
   B. He was formal and distant.
   C. He was gentle and surprisingly delicate.
   D. He was eager and overly familiar.
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

26. What did Henchard do after Elizabeth-Jane left the room?
   A. He sat stiffly, gazing at the wall.
   B. He immediately left to go meet Susan Newson.
   C. He began to write another letter.
   D. He resumed his work with Donald Farfrae.
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

27. What did Henchard mean when he said "It never rains but it pours!"?
   A. He was happy about the sudden turn of events.
   B. He was overwhelmed by the unexpected news.
   C. He was frustrated by the interruption to his work.
   D. He was concerned about potential problems.
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

28. Why did Susan Newson not read Henchard's note immediately?
   A. She was busy with other tasks.
   B. She wanted Elizabeth-Jane to describe the meeting first.
   C. She was suspicious of the contents.
   D. She wanted to keep the contents a secret from Elizabeth-Jane.
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

29. What did the amount of five guineas enclosed in Henchard's note likely signify?
   A. It was a generous gift to help Susan Newson.
   B. It was payment for some past debt or obligation.
   C. It was a bribe to keep Elizabeth-Jane unaware of the situation.
   D. It was intended as recompense for some wrongdoing.
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

30. How did the text describe Henchard's physical appearance, particularly his eyes?
   A. His eyes had a piercing, intense gaze.
   B. His eyes had a red spark of light in them.
   C. His eyes were large and expressive.
   D. His eyes were dull and lifeless.
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

## Set: 12

# Set: 12

Q1: What was the name of the historical Roman structure described in the passage?

A) The Coliseum

B) The Amphitheatre

C) The Arena

D) The Circus

Answer: B

Q2: What was the local name for the Roman Amphitheatre in Casterbridge?

A) The Ring

B) The Spittoon

C) The Ruin

D) The Enclosure

Answer: A

Q3: What was found when digging in the town fields and gardens around Casterbridge?

A) Ancient Roman artifacts

B) Buried treasure

C) The remains of Roman soldiers

D) Nothing of significance

Answer: C

Q4: How did the passage describe the interior form of the Amphitheatre?

A) Circular and vast

B) Sloping and bowl-shaped

C) Rectangular and ruined

D) Tiered and multilevel

Answer: B

Q5: What was one of the common uses for the Amphitheatre in Casterbridge?

A) A public market

B) A place for executions

C) A setting for romantic trysts

D) A venue for sporting events

Answer: B

Q6: What unusual phenomenon was reported to have been seen by people sitting in the Amphitheatre?

A) Ghostly Roman soldiers

B) Visions of gladiatorial combat

C) Mysterious underground tunnels

D) Strange animal noises

Answer: B

Q7: What was discovered to still remain under the south entrance of the Amphitheatre?

A) Excavated cells for wild animals and athletes

B) An ancient pagan altar

C) Underground tunnels leading from the town

D) The ruins of a medieval chapel

Answer: A

Q8: Why did the passage suggest that the Amphitheatre was a poor location for lovers' meetings?

A) It was too public and crowded.

B) It had a sinister and tragic history.

C) It was too remote and inaccessible.

D) The atmosphere was distracting.

Answer: B

Q9: How did the passage describe the appearance of the Amphitheatre in the evening dusk?

A) Bright and inviting

B) Dark and foreboding

C) Melancholy and impressive

D) Bustling and lively

Answer: C

Q10: What sport did some local boys try to play in the Amphitheatre's central arena?

A) Football

B) Cricket

C) Wrestling

D) Jousting

Answer: B

Q11: Why did the boys' attempts at playing games in the Amphitheatre usually "languish"?

A) The grass was too long.

B) The arena was too hot during the day.

C) The privacy and seclusion was dismal.

D) The local authorities banned such activities.

Answer: C

Q12: Why did Henchard choose the Amphitheatre as the meeting place for his long-lost wife Susan?

A) It was a private and secluded location.

B) It was a public place they could easily find.

C) It was a neutral ground for their reunion.

D) All of the above.

Answer: D

Q13: What did Henchard say to Susan about his drinking habits when they first met?

A) He assured her he no longer drank.

B) He apologized for his past drinking.

C) He promised to stop drinking for her.

D) He claimed he had never been a heavy drinker.

Answer: A

Q14: Why did Susan believe she could not abandon the man she had married after he had "paid so much" for her?

A) She felt a sense of moral obligation.

B) She was afraid of the social consequences.

C) She still loved her late husband.

D) She wanted to protect her daughter.

Answer: A

Q15: What plan did Henchard propose to Susan for their future living situation?

A) For them to live openly as husband and wife.

B) For Susan and Elizabeth-Jane to live separately.

C) For them to remarry and present Elizabeth-Jane as Henchard's stepdaughter.

D) For Susan and Elizabeth-Jane to leave Casterbridge.

Answer: C

Q16: Why did Henchard feel it was necessary to keep the true nature of his relationship with Susan and Elizabeth-Jane a secret?

A) To protect his position and reputation in the town.

B) To avoid burdening Elizabeth-Jane with the truth.

C) To prevent Susan from abandoning him again.

D) Both A and B.

Answer: D

Q17: Where did Henchard suggest Susan and Elizabeth-Jane should temporarily stay while his plan was being arranged?

A) At the Three Mariners Inn

B) In a cottage in the town

C) In lodgings over the china-shop

D) At Henchard's own house

Answer: C

Q18: What did Henchard tell Susan about providing for her financially?

A) He would give her a monthly allowance.

B) He would pay for their new lodgings.

C) He offered to lend her money until he returned.

D) He promised to support her and Elizabeth-Jane.

Answer: D

Q19: What was Henchard's main concern about Elizabeth-Jane potentially discovering the truth?

A) That she would be ashamed of their past.

B) That she would run away from them.

C) That she would refuse to accept him.

D) That she would tell others about it.

Answer: A

Q20: How did Susan respond when Henchard asked if she forgave him?

A) She immediately forgave him.

B) She said she could not forgive him.

C) She hesitated and seemed to find it difficult to answer.

D) She said she would need time to decide.

Answer: C

Q21: What did the passage say about the Amphitheatre's history of executions?

A) A woman was burned there for murdering her husband.

B) Prisoners were publicly hanged there for many years.

C) Both public executions and violent crimes took place there.

D) The details of the executions were unknown.

Answer: A

Q22: How did the passage describe the Amphitheatre's relationship to the town of Casterbridge?

A) It was a remote and forgotten relic.

B) It was a popular tourist attraction.

C) It was an integral part of the town's identity.

D) It was a source of shame and embarrassment.

Answer: C

Q23: What did the passage say the Amphitheatre's "history proved" about its past associations?

A) It had been a site of celebration and festivity.

B) It had been a venue for noble and heroic deeds.

C) It had been a location for sinister and tragic events.

D) It had been an important center of commerce and trade.

Answer: C

Q24: How did the passage describe the appearance of the Amphitheatre's central arena?

A) Smooth and circular, as if recently used.

B) Overgrown with grass and weeds.

C) Filled with the remains of its former structures.

D) Transformed into a cricket pitch for local boys.

Answer: A

Q25: What did the passage say was one reason the cricket games in the Amphitheatre "languished"?

A) The players were intimidated by the sinister history.

B) The grass was too long and uneven.

D) The games were banned by the local authorities.

D) The privacy and seclusion was oppressive.

Answer: D

Q26: What did the passage say was the "proper hour" to get a true impression of the Amphitheatre's "real vastness"?

A) Midday

B) Sunset

C) Twilight

D) Nightfall

Answer: C

Q27: What detail did the passage provide about the excavated cells under the south entrance of the Amphitheatre?

A) They were used to hold wild animals.

B) They were used to hold athletes and gladiators.

C) They were connected by underground tunnels.

D) Both A and B.

Answer: D

Q28: How did Henchard describe his "shady, headstrong, disgraceful life as a young man" in relation to his plan with Susan?

A) He was ashamed of it and wanted to keep it secret.

B) He was proud of it and wanted to openly acknowledge it.

C) He felt it was an important part of his past.

D) He believed it would not impact their new life together.

Answer: A

Q29: Why did Susan say she would have never come to see Henchard if her late husband had not died?

A) She did not want to betray her husband's memory.

B) She was afraid of Henchard's reaction.

C) She believed she owed her husband lifelong faithfulness.

D) Both A and C.

Answer: D

Q30: What did the passage say was the one type of "appointment" that "seldom had place" in the Amphitheatre?

A) Business meetings

B) Public gatherings

C) Happy lovers' trysts

D) Political rallies

Answer: C

   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

29. Why did Susan say she would have never come to see Henchard if her late husband had not died?
   A. She did not want to betray her husband's memory.
   B. She was afraid of Henchard's reaction.
   C. She believed she owed her husband lifelong faithfulness.
   D. Both A and C.
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

30. What did the passage say was the one type of "appointment" that "seldom had place" in the Amphitheatre?
   A. Business meetings
   B. Public gatherings
   C. Happy lovers' trysts
   D. Political rallies
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

D) 19 years

D) All of the above

Answer: D

C) Prosperous and busy

D) Uneventful and routine

Answer: B

D) A morally upright citizen

Answer: A

Answer: C

Answer: B

C) Fishing and seafood

D) Grain and cereal crops

Answer: C

B) Providing financially for them

C) Keeping his past a secret from his daughter

D) Convincing his first wife to remarry him

Answer: C

B) Her husband

C) Her uncle

D) Not specified

## Set: 14

# Set: 14

Q1: Where was the cottage that Henchard hired for his wife Susan located?

A) In the center of town

B) Near the Roman wall

C) In the countryside

D) In the poorest part of town

Answer: B

Q2: What was the general tone of the conversation when Henchard visited Susan and Elizabeth?

A) Casual and friendly

B) Serious and businesslike

C) Tense and uncomfortable

D) Carefully guarded

Answer: D

Q3: How did Henchard's visits to Susan and Elizabeth affect the town's perception of him?

A) They were seen as a scandal

B) They were seen as a sign of his charity

C) They were seen as proof of his commitment to his family

D) They were seen as a sign of his weakness

Answer: A

Q4: What was Elizabeth-Jane's reaction to Henchard's visits to her and her mother?

A) She was happy and excited

B) She was suspicious and uncomfortable

C) She was unaware of the true nature of the relationship

D) She resented the visits

Answer: C

Q5: What was Susan Henchard's initial reaction to Henchard's efforts to provide for her and Elizabeth-Jane?

A) She was grateful and appreciative

B) She was overwhelmed and uncomfortable

C) She was suspicious of his motives

D) She refused to accept his help

Answer: B

Q6: What was Henchard's primary motivation for remarrying Susan?

A) To provide a stable home for Elizabeth-Jane

B) To repair the damage from his past mistakes

C) To improve his public reputation

D) All of the above

Answer: D

Q7: Who was the only person present at the wedding who knew the true situation between Henchard, Susan, and Elizabeth-Jane?

A) Christopher Coney

B) Farfrae

C) Mrs. Cuxsom

D) Nance Mockridge

Answer: B

Q8: What was the general opinion of the townspeople regarding Henchard's decision to marry Susan?

A) They thought it was a wise and sensible decision

B) They were indifferent to the situation

C) They found it puzzling and out of character

D) They condemned it as a scandal

Answer: C

Q9: How did the weather on the day of Henchard and Susan's wedding affect the scene?

A) It was a bright, sunny day

B) It was a cold, windy day

C) It was a warm, rainy day

D) It was a foggy, misty day

Answer: C

Q10: What did the townspeople say about Henchard's past as a "parish 'prentice"?

A) They were unaware of it

B) They thought it was a rumor

C) They did not think it was relevant

D) They saw it as a sign of his humble origins

Answer: D

Q11: What was the significance of the town's nickname for Susan, "The Ghost"?

A) It reflected her fragile appearance

B) It suggested she was an unwelcome presence

C) It was a sign of the town's disrespect for her

D) All of the above

Answer: A

Q12: How did Henchard react when he overheard the townspeople referring to Susan as "The Ghost"?

A) He laughed it off

B) He ignored it

C) He became angry and defensive

D) He tried to correct the rumor

Answer: C

Q13: What was the attitude of the town's people towards Henchard's marriage to Susan?

A) They were excited and supportive

B) They were indifferent and uninterested

C) They were confused and suspicious

D) They were openly critical and judgmental

Answer: C

Q14: What was the relationship between Mrs. Cuxsom and Christopher Coney?

B) They were distant acquaintances

C) They were family members

D) They were romantic partners

Answer: A

Q15: What did the townspeople find most puzzling about Henchard's decision to marry Susan?

A) Her low social status

B) Her advanced age

C) Her fragile health

D) His long-standing indifference to women

Answer: D

A) He was more attentive and affectionate

B) He was more aloof and distant

C) He was more controlling and domineering

D) He was more indifferent and dismissive

Answer: A

B) To maintain control and surveillance over them

C) To keep up appearances in the town

D) All of the above

Answer: D

B) She was poor and socially isolated

C) She was of middling means and status

D) Her status was not discussed

Answer: B

B) Gossipy and judgmental

C) Lighthearted and amused

D) Indifferent and detached

Answer: B

Answer: D

D) It was a gesture to maintain their privacy

Answer: C

D) They did not have any information about it

   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

   D. They were seen as a sign of his weakness

5. What was Susan Henchard's initial reaction to Henchard's efforts to provide for her and Elizabeth-Jane?

   C. She was suspicious of his motives
   D. She refused to accept his help
   **उत्तर:** B

6. What was Henchard's primary motivation for remarrying Susan?
   A. To provide a stable home for Elizabeth-Jane
   B. To repair the damage from his past mistakes
   C. To improve his public reputation
   D. All of the above
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

7. Who was the only person present at the wedding who knew the true situation between Henchard, Susan, and Elizabeth-Jane?

   C. Mrs. Cuxsom
   D. Nance Mockridge
   **उत्तर:** B

8. What was the general opinion of the townspeople regarding Henchard's decision to marry Susan?

   C. They found it puzzling and out of character
   D. They condemned it as a scandal
   **उत्तर:** C

9. How did the weather on the day of Henchard and Susan's wedding affect the scene?

   C. It was a warm, rainy day
   D. It was a foggy, misty day
   **उत्तर:** C

10. What did the townspeople say about Henchard's past as a "parish 'prentice"?
   A. They were unaware of it
   B. They thought it was a rumor
   C. They did not think it was relevant
   D. They saw it as a sign of his humble origins
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

11. What was the significance of the town's nickname for Susan, "The Ghost"?
   A. It reflected her fragile appearance
   B. It suggested she was an unwelcome presence
   C. It was a sign of the town's disrespect for her
   D. All of the above
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

12. How did Henchard react when he overheard the townspeople referring to Susan as "The Ghost"?

   B. He ignored it

   D. He tried to correct the rumor
   **उत्तर:** C

   **उत्तर:** C

   **उत्तर:** A

15. What did the townspeople find most puzzling about Henchard's decision to marry Susan?

   B. Her advanced age
   C. Her fragile health
   D. His long-standing indifference to women
   **उत्तर:** D

   **उत्तर:** B

   **उत्तर:** B

   D. It was an idiom referring to his stubborn nature

   **उत्तर:** A

   **उत्तर:** C

# Set: 15

Q1: What color did Henchard have the iron railings painted?

A) Bright red

B) Dull rust

C) Bright green

D) White

Answer: C

Q2: How many coats of white paint did Henchard have applied to the windows?

A) One

B) Two

C) Three

D) Four

Answer: C

Q3: What change did Elizabeth-Jane experience as a result of the abundance of good things?

A) Her skin became muddier

B) Her cheek developed a bloom

C) Her brow became more lined

D) Her eyes became less thoughtful

Answer: B

Q4: What was Elizabeth-Jane's attitude towards light-heartedness?

A) She indulged in it frequently

B) She avoided it completely

C) She enjoyed it in moderation

D) She considered it irrational and inconsequent

Answer: D

Q5: How did Elizabeth-Jane refrain from making a fool of herself by dress?

A) By an innate perceptiveness

B) By following her mother's advice

C) By imitating the Casterbridge girls

D) By avoiding new clothes altogether

Answer: A

Q6: What line did Elizabeth-Jane draw when it came to her sunshade?

A) She preferred it with fringe

B) She wanted it plain edged

C) She insisted on an ivory handle

D) She desired a bright color

Answer: B

Q7: Why did Elizabeth-Jane start protecting her cheeks from the sun?

A) To maintain her spotless complexion

B) To avoid looking too feminine

C) To keep up with the latest fashion

D) To hide her freckles

Answer: A

Q8: How did Henchard look at Elizabeth-Jane's head of hair at breakfast?

A) Admiringly

B) Critically

C) Silently

D) Disapprovingly

Answer: C

Q9: What was the color of Elizabeth-Jane's hair?

A) Black

B) Light brown

C) Dark brown

D) Blonde

Answer: B

Q10: What did Henchard want to change about Elizabeth-Jane?

A) Her hair color

B) Her style of dress

C) Her surname

D) Her circle of friends

Answer: C

Q11: How did Elizabeth-Jane react to Henchard's proposal to change her surname?

A) She agreed immediately

B) She refused outright

C) She asked for time to think about it

D) She consulted her mother first

Answer: C

Q12: What system did Henchard use for his corn and hay business before Farfrae's management?

A) A computerized system

B) A written ledger system

C) A crude vivâ voce system

D) An outsourced management system

Answer: C

Q13: How did Henchard treat Farfrae when they walked together?

A) As a younger brother

B) As a business rival

C) As a subordinate employee

D) As an equal partner

Answer: A

Q14: What did Elizabeth-Jane observe about Henchard's affection for Farfrae?

A) It was consistently respectful

B) It occasionally turned domineering

C) It was purely professional

D) It was insincere and manipulative

Answer: B

Q15: Why did Elizabeth-Jane think Farfrae looked at her and her mother with curious interest?

A) He found them attractive

B) He knew about their past

C) He wanted to befriend them

D) He was just turning his eyes

Answer: D

Q16: What did Elizabeth-Jane conjecture about Henchard and her mother's past relationship?

A) They were secret lovers

B) They had a business partnership

C) They might have been lovers who quarreled and parted

D) They were sworn enemies

Answer: C

Q17: How was Casterbridge positioned in relation to the surrounding farmland?

A) It was integrated with a transitional intermixture

B) It was clean-cut and distinct

C) It was separated by a wide suburban area

D) It was indistinguishable from the countryside

Answer: B

Q18: From where could the farmer's boy pitch a stone into the town-clerk's office window?

A) The granary roof

B) The church tower

C) The barley-mow

D) The meadow

Answer: C

Q19: Where did the crowd stand during executions in Casterbridge?

A) In the town square

B) On the pavement-corner

C) In a meadow before the drop

D) Inside the prison yard

Answer: C

Q20: What was the eastern purlieu of Casterbridge where the upland farmers lived called?

A) Durnover

B) Kingsbere

C) Weatherbury

D) Mellstock

Answer: A

Q21: What was unique about the street in the Durnover end of Casterbridge?

A) It had nothing urban about it

B) It was exclusively residential

C) It was filled with industrial warehouses

D) It was a recently developed area

Answer: A

Q22: Where was the granary located in which Elizabeth-Jane was asked to meet someone?

A) On Durnover Hill

B) Next to Henchard's house

C) In the town center

D) By the Roman ruins

Answer: A

Q23: Why did Elizabeth-Jane ascend the granary step-ladder before Farfrae saw her?

A) To hide from him out of shyness

B) To get a better view of the surrounding farmland

C) To check the quality of the stored grains

D) To see if Henchard was inside

Answer: A

Q24: What did Elizabeth-Jane do to relieve her suspense while waiting in the granary?

A) She paced back and forth

B) She peered out of the small window

C) She gently moved the winnowing machine handle

D) She rearranged the corn sacks

Answer: C

Q25: What happened when Elizabeth-Jane moved the winnowing machine handle?

A) The machine started loudly

B) A cloud of wheat husks flew into her face

C) The granary door swung open

D) Farfrae immediately noticed her

Answer: B

Q26: What did Elizabeth-Jane and Farfrae realize about the notes asking them to come to the granary?

A) They were meant for two other people

B) They were part of a trick played on them

C) They indicated an emergency situation

D) They were secretly written by Henchard

Answer: B

Q27: How did Farfrae feel about the song he sang at the Three Mariners?

A) He regretted singing it

B) He didn't think much of it afterwards

C) He felt it deeply expressed his feelings

D) He wished to forget about it

Answer: B

Q28: What did Farfrae notice about Elizabeth-Jane's appearance in the granary?

A) There were husks and dust on her clothes

B) Her bonnet was askew

C) Her hair was disheveled

D) Her dress was wet from the rain

Answer: A

Q29: How did Farfrae help clean Elizabeth-Jane's clothes in the granary?

A) He brushed them with his hands

B) He used the winnowing machine

C) He blew on them

D) He shook them vigorously

Answer: C

Q30: What did Farfrae whistle as Elizabeth-Jane walked away from the granary?

A) A Scottish folk tune

B) A psalm melody

C) A popular operatic aria

D) A sea shanty

Answer: A

   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

17. How was Casterbridge positioned in relation to the surrounding farmland?
   A. It was integrated with a transitional intermixture
   B. It was clean-cut and distinct
   C. It was separated by a wide suburban area
   D. It was indistinguishable from the countryside
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

18. From where could the farmer's boy pitch a stone into the town-clerk's office window?
   A. The granary roof
   B. The church tower
   C. The barley-mow
   D. The meadow
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

19. Where did the crowd stand during executions in Casterbridge?
   A. In the town square
   B. On the pavement-corner
   C. In a meadow before the drop
   D. Inside the prison yard
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

20. What was the eastern purlieu of Casterbridge where the upland farmers lived called?
   A. Durnover
   B. Kingsbere
   C. Weatherbury
   D. Mellstock
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

21. What was unique about the street in the Durnover end of Casterbridge?
   A. It had nothing urban about it
   B. It was exclusively residential
   C. It was filled with industrial warehouses
   D. It was a recently developed area
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

22. Where was the granary located in which Elizabeth-Jane was asked to meet someone?
   A. On Durnover Hill
   B. Next to Henchard's house
   C. In the town center
   D. By the Roman ruins
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

23. Why did Elizabeth-Jane ascend the granary step-ladder before Farfrae saw her?
   A. To hide from him out of shyness
   B. To get a better view of the surrounding farmland
   C. To check the quality of the stored grains
   D. To see if Henchard was inside
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

24. What did Elizabeth-Jane do to relieve her suspense while waiting in the granary?
   A. She paced back and forth
   B. She peered out of the small window
   C. She gently moved the winnowing machine handle
   D. She rearranged the corn sacks
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

25. What happened when Elizabeth-Jane moved the winnowing machine handle?
   A. The machine started loudly
   B. A cloud of wheat husks flew into her face
   C. The granary door swung open
   D. Farfrae immediately noticed her
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

26. What did Elizabeth-Jane and Farfrae realize about the notes asking them to come to the granary?
   A. They were meant for two other people
   B. They were part of a trick played on them
   C. They indicated an emergency situation
   D. They were secretly written by Henchard
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

27. How did Farfrae feel about the song he sang at the Three Mariners?
   A. He regretted singing it
   B. He didn't think much of it afterwards
   C. He felt it deeply expressed his feelings
   D. He wished to forget about it
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

28. What did Farfrae notice about Elizabeth-Jane's appearance in the granary?
   A. There were husks and dust on her clothes
   B. Her bonnet was askew
   C. Her hair was disheveled
   D. Her dress was wet from the rain
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

29. How did Farfrae help clean Elizabeth-Jane's clothes in the granary?
   A. He brushed them with his hands
   B. He used the winnowing machine
   C. He blew on them
   D. He shook them vigorously
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

30. What did Farfrae whistle as Elizabeth-Jane walked away from the granary?
   A. A Scottish folk tune
   B. A psalm melody
   C. A popular operatic aria
   D. A sea shanty
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

## Set: 16

# Set: 16

Q1: What was the relationship between Elizabeth-Jane and Henchard?

A) Elizabeth-Jane was Henchard's stepdaughter.

B) Elizabeth-Jane was Henchard's daughter.

C) Elizabeth-Jane was Henchard's niece.

D) Elizabeth-Jane was not related to Henchard.

Answer: A

Q2: Why did Elizabeth-Jane start buying more elaborate clothing?

A) She wanted to impress Donald Farfrae.

B) She wanted to fit in with the townspeople.

C) She was trying to conceal her past life.

D) She was indulging her newfound wealth.

Answer: D

Q3: Why was Abel Whittle often late to work?

A) He liked to sleep in.

B) He had trouble waking up on time.

C) He had to take care of his sick mother.

D) He enjoyed annoying Henchard.

Answer: B

Q4: What did Henchard threaten to do to Abel Whittle if he was late again?

A) Dismiss him from his job.

B) Dock his pay.

C) Physically punish him.

D) Publicly humiliate him.

Answer: C

Q5: How did Farfrae react when he saw Henchard forcing Abel Whittle to go to work without his breeches?

A) He laughed at the situation.

B) He ignored it and went about his business.

C) He scolded Henchard for his behavior.

D) He intervened and insisted that Abel be allowed to get dressed.

Answer: D

Q6: What did the people in Durnover say about Farfrae compared to Henchard?

A) Farfrae was more hardworking and diligent.

B) Farfrae was more intelligent and knowledgeable.

C) Farfrae was more temperamental and emotional.

D) Farfrae was more charismatic and likable.

Answer: D

Q7: What was the reason Henchard was upset when Farfrae intervened in the situation with Abel Whittle?

A) Henchard was embarrassed that Farfrae had seen his harsh treatment of Abel.

B) Henchard felt that Farfrae was undermining his authority as the boss.

C) Henchard was concerned that Farfrae was gaining the loyalty of the workers.

D) Henchard was worried that Farfrae was becoming too influential in the town.

Answer: B

Q8: What job did Abel Whittle often have to do for Henchard?

A) Weigh the corn.

B) Accompany the wagons to the country.

C) Operate the crane.

D) All of the above.

Answer: D

Q9: How did Elizabeth-Jane feel about the attention she was receiving for her newfound beauty?

A) She was proud and enjoyed the admiration.

B) She was uncomfortable and felt it was undeserved.

C) She was indifferent and didn't care about it.

D) She was confused and didn't understand why she was being noticed.

Answer: B

Q10: What did Elizabeth-Jane realize was happening between Henchard and Farfrae?

A) They were becoming close friends.

B) They were secretly plotting against each other.

C) They were developing a rivalry.

D) They were growing apart and becoming distant.

Answer: A

Q11: Why did Henchard refuse to go with Farfrae to assess the value of the hay?

A) He was too busy with a Town Council meeting.

B) He didn't want to be in Farfrae's company.

C) He felt Farfrae was better suited for the task.

D) He was still upset from his earlier confrontation with Farfrae.

Answer: C

Q12: What did Henchard mean when he said he had "told ye the secret o' my life" to Farfrae?

A) He had revealed his past criminal history to Farfrae.

B) He had confided in Farfrae about his troubled relationship with his late wife.

C) He had shared details about his past business failures with Farfrae.

D) He had disclosed personal information about his early life to Farfrae.

Answer: D

Q13: What was the reason Henchard was upset when the child messenger said the people in Durnover preferred Farfrae to him?

A) He was jealous of Farfrae's popularity.

B) He felt his authority as the corn-factor was being undermined.

C) He was hurt that the townspeople no longer respected him.

D) All of the above.

Answer: D

Q14: What did Henchard mean when he said to Farfrae, "Ah—I know why! I've told ye the secret o' my life—fool that I was to do't—and you take advantage of me!"?

A) Henchard felt Farfrae was using the personal information against him.

B) Henchard regretted confiding in Farfrae about his past.

C) Henchard believed Farfrae was betraying his trust.

D) All of the above.

Answer: D

Q15: What was the reason Farfrae was reluctant to continue assessing the value of the hay when he met Henchard on the way?

A) He didn't want to intrude on Henchard's business.

B) He felt it would be disrespectful to the bereaved family.

C) He was concerned about Henchard's emotional state.

D) He didn't have the necessary expertise to evaluate the hay.

Answer: B

Q16: What was Henchard's reaction when the child messenger said the people in Durnover preferred Farfrae to him?

A) He became angry and defensive.

B) He brushed it off as meaningless gossip.

C) He was hurt and felt betrayed by the townspeople.

D) He responded with a mix of resignation and bitterness.

Answer: D

Q17: Why did Henchard initially refuse to go with Farfrae to assess the value of the hay?

A) He was busy with a Town Council meeting.

B) He didn't want to be in Farfrae's company.

C) He felt Farfrae was more qualified for the task.

D) He was still upset from his earlier confrontation with Farfrae.

Answer: A

Q18: What was the reason Henchard felt Farfrae was taking advantage of him after telling him the "secret o' my life"?

A) Henchard believed Farfrae was using the information against him.

B) Henchard regretted confiding in Farfrae about his past.

C) Henchard felt Farfrae was betraying his trust.

D) All of the above.

Answer: D

Q19: How did Farfrae respond when Henchard accused him of taking advantage of the personal information he had shared?

A) Farfrae denied having any such intention.

B) Farfrae apologized for any misunderstanding.

C) Farfrae said he had forgotten the information Henchard had shared.

D) Farfrae remained silent and did not engage with Henchard's accusation.

Answer: C

Q20: What was the "secret o' my life" that Henchard had told Farfrae, according to the text?

A) Henchard's past criminal history.

B) Henchard's troubled relationship with his late wife.

C) Henchard's past business failures.

D) Personal details about Henchard's early life.

Answer: D

Q21: Why did Henchard feel that Farfrae was becoming the more admired man in Casterbridge?

B) Farfrae was more intelligent and knowledgeable.

C) Farfrae was more charismatic and likable.

D) All of the above.

Answer: D

23. What did Henchard mean when he said to Farfrae, "Why did you speak to me before them like that, Farfrae? You might have stopped till we were alone. Ah—I know why! I've told ye the secret o' my life—fool that I was to do't—and you take advantage of me!"?

   C. Henchard believed Farfrae was betraying his trust.

# Set: 18

Q1: What was Elizabeth-Jane's reaction to Farfrae's invitation to dance?

A) She was excited to dance with him.

B) She felt she was not in her proper place to dance with him.

C) She politely declined the invitation.

D) She was indifferent to the invitation.

Answer: B

Q2: Why did Elizabeth-Jane start running home after parting ways with Farfrae?

A) She was embarrassed by their conversation.

B) She wanted to avoid her father seeing her with Farfrae.

C) She was excited to share the news of Farfrae's potential departure.

D) She realized she had forgotten something at home.

Answer: A

A) She copied the letter and addressed it to him.

B) She threw the letter away without reading it.

C) She showed the letter to her father.

D) She carefully studied the letter's contents.

Answer: A

Q4: What was Henchard's reaction to Farfrae starting his own corn and hay business?

A) He was pleased that Farfrae was pursuing his own venture.

B) He was indifferent and did not care about Farfrae's business.

C) He was upset and saw Farfrae as an enemy.

D) He tried to convince Farfrae to join his business instead.

Answer: C

Q5: Why did Farfrae refuse to take on a customer who had previously dealt with Henchard?

A) Farfrae wanted to avoid competing with Henchard.

B) Farfrae was concerned about the customer's reputation.

C) Farfrae wanted to maintain his friendship with Henchard.

D) Farfrae was worried the customer would not pay.

Answer: C

A) That she continue her friendly interactions with Farfrae.

C) That she promise to have no further contact with Farfrae.

D) That she try to convince Farfrae to leave the town.

Answer: C

C) She asked her mother for advice on how to gauge Farfrae's feelings.

D) They tried to undermine each other's reputations in the town.

Q1: What was the initial reason for Mrs. Henchard sending the note asking Elizabeth-Jane and Farfrae to meet in Durnover Barton?

B) To bring Elizabeth-Jane and Farfrae together.

C) To test their feelings for each other.

D) To set up a meeting between Henchard and Farfrae.

Answer: B

Q2: What did Mrs. Henchard request be done with her belongings after her death?

A) She requested that all her belongings be burned.

B) She requested that all her possessions be donated to charity.

C) She requested that specific items be kept and used as she directed.

D) She did not provide any specific requests about her belongings.

Answer: C

Q3: Why did Christopher Coney dig up and spend the ounce pennies that Mrs. Henchard had requested be buried?

A) He believed the pennies rightfully belonged to him.

B) He wanted to use the money to buy drinks at the Three Mariners.

C) He did not believe the dead should claim ownership of money.

D) He was unaware of Mrs. Henchard's request for the pennies to be buried.

Answer: C

C) She was indifferent and did not care about her mother's motivations.

D) She was relieved that her mother had been involved in bringing her and Farfrae together.

Answer: B

B) She requested they be left closed.

C) She requested they be opened to make the room cheerful.

D) She did not provide any specific requests about the windows.

D) She wanted to prevent Henchard's current wife from finding out.

# Set: 20

Q1: What was Henchard's relationship to Elizabeth-Jane?

A) Henchard was Elizabeth-Jane's stepfather.

B) Henchard was Elizabeth-Jane's biological father.

C) Henchard was a close family friend of Elizabeth-Jane.

D) Henchard had no direct relation to Elizabeth-Jane.

Answer: B

Q2: What was the reason behind Elizabeth-Jane's mother keeping Henchard's paternity a secret?

A) She wanted to protect Elizabeth-Jane from Henchard's troubled past.

B) She was angry with Henchard and wanted to punish him.

C) She wanted Elizabeth-Jane to have a better life with Richard Newson.

D) She was afraid Henchard would not accept Elizabeth-Jane as his daughter.

Answer: C

Q3: Why did Henchard decide to reveal his paternity to Elizabeth-Jane?

A) He wanted to get revenge on his wife for keeping the truth from him.

B) He felt guilty about abandoning Elizabeth-Jane and wanted to make amends.

C) He wanted Elizabeth-Jane to take his surname and be legally recognized as his daughter.

D) He could no longer bear the pain of keeping the truth from her.

Answer: D

Q4: How did Elizabeth-Jane react to Henchard's revelation?

A) She was overjoyed and immediately accepted him as her father.

B) She was skeptical and demanded proof of his claims.

C) She was troubled and upset by the sudden news.

D) She did not believe him and accused him of lying.

Answer: C

Q5: What was the "strange mistake" Elizabeth-Jane referred to regarding her mother's marriage to Richard Newson?

A) Newson had tricked her mother into marrying him.

B) Her mother had accidentally married the wrong man.

C) Newson had married Elizabeth-Jane's mother while Henchard was still alive.

D) Newson had taken advantage of Elizabeth-Jane's mother's vulnerability.

Answer: C

Q6: How did Henchard feel about the revelation of Elizabeth-Jane's true parentage?

A) He was relieved and happy to have his daughter back.

B) He was angry and blamed his wife for the deception.

C) He was indifferent and saw it as an inconvenience.

D) He was devastated and felt that he had been betrayed.

Answer: D

Q7: Why was Henchard's wife reluctant to have Elizabeth-Jane's name changed to Henchard?

A) She wanted to protect Elizabeth-Jane from Henchard's reputation.

B) She was still in love with Richard Newson and wanted to honor his memory.

C) She believed Elizabeth-Jane would be better off with the Newson name.

D) She felt guilty about the deception and wanted to maintain the status quo.

Answer: C

Q8: What did Henchard do after learning the truth about Elizabeth-Jane's parentage?

A) He immediately confessed the truth to Elizabeth-Jane.

B) He confronted his wife's grave and cursed her.

C) He decided to keep the truth hidden and continue the charade.

D) He left town to avoid facing the consequences of the revelation.

Answer: C

Q9: How did Elizabeth-Jane's physical appearance compare to Henchard's?

A) They had similar features, suggesting a close biological connection.

B) Their physical appearances were markedly different, with Henchard being darker.

C) Henchard could not discern any resemblance between them.

D) Elizabeth-Jane's appearance was a perfect blend of both Henchard and Newson.

Answer: B

Q10: What was the significance of the letter Henchard found from his late wife?

A) It revealed the truth about Elizabeth-Jane's paternity.

B) It contained instructions for Henchard on how to care for Elizabeth-Jane.

C) It expressed his wife's final wishes and forgiveness for Henchard.

D) It provided details about Elizabeth-Jane's upbringing with Newson.

Answer: A

Q11: What was the meaning behind the "strange concatenation of events" that angered Henchard?

A) He felt that the revelations were the result of a cruel twist of fate.

B) He believed there was a sinister scheme orchestrated against him.

C) He was frustrated that his efforts to claim Elizabeth-Jane as his daughter were undermined.

D) He was confused by the series of events that led to the discovery of the truth.

Answer: C

Q12: Where did Henchard go after the emotional revelation to Elizabeth-Jane?

A) He went to the local pub to drown his sorrows.

B) He went for a walk along the river, reflecting on the situation.

C) He locked himself in his room, refusing to see anyone.

D) He left town, unable to face the consequences of his actions.

Answer: B

Q13: What was the significance of the location Henchard walked to along the river?

A) It was a place of execution, reflecting the dark nature of his situation.

B) It was a peaceful and serene spot, where he could find solace.

C) It was a bustling area of the town, where he could be distracted from his troubles.

D) It was a place he and his wife had often visited together in happier times.

Answer: A

## Set: 21

# Set: 21

Q1: What was the reason for Henchard's sharp reprimand of Elizabeth-Jane's use of dialect words?

A) He wanted her to speak more formally to reflect his social status.

B) He was embarrassed by her use of common language in front of others.

C) He believed she should use more sophisticated language as his daughter.

D) He wanted to discourage her from using words he considered vulgar.

Answer: C

Q2: How did Elizabeth-Jane's handwriting style differ from Henchard's preference?

A) Henchard preferred a delicate, "ladies'-hand" style, while Elizabeth's was bold and strong.

B) Elizabeth's handwriting was more refined and elegant than Henchard's preference.

C) Henchard demanded Elizabeth write in a casual, informal style, while she preferred a formal hand.

D) Henchard's preferred handwriting style was difficult to read, while Elizabeth's was clear and legible.

Answer: A

Q3: Why did Henchard become angry with Elizabeth-Jane for bringing a food offering to Nance Mockridge?

A) He was concerned it would make Elizabeth appear as a servant.

B) He did not want Elizabeth associating with a woman of Nance's character.

C) He was embarrassed that Elizabeth was performing such a lowly task.

D) All of the above.

Answer: D

Q4: What was the revelation to Elizabeth-Jane about the young lady she saw in the churchyard?

A) The lady was more beautifully dressed than Elizabeth.

B) The lady's gait and appearance were more refined than Elizabeth's.

C) Elizabeth had never before seen a woman with such external development.

D) All of the above.

Answer: D

Q5: What was the impact on Henchard of learning that Elizabeth-Jane had worked as a waitress at the Three Mariners?

A) He was proud that she had taken on such work.

B) He considered it a social catastrophe that damaged his reputation.

C) He saw it as a practical necessity and did not judge her for it.

D) He was indifferent to the news, as it did not affect him personally.

Answer: B

Q6: What was Henchard's reason for withdrawing his objection to Farfrae courting Elizabeth-Jane?

A) He genuinely wanted the two to be together.

B) He hoped it would remove Elizabeth-Jane from his household.

C) He thought it would bring him a marriage portion from Farfrae.

D) Both B and C.

Answer: D

Q7: How did Elizabeth-Jane react to seeing Farfrae pass by in the churchyard?

A) She was excited and waved to him.

B) She felt he had scorned her and was depressed.

C) She was indifferent to his presence.

D) She confronted him about his dismissal.

Answer: B

Q8: What was the lady's initial reaction when she overheard Elizabeth-Jane's comment about wishing she was dead with her mother?

A) She was shocked and scolded Elizabeth.

B) She was sympathetic and inquired about Elizabeth's situation.

C) She ignored the comment and changed the subject.

D) She was curious and asked Elizabeth to elaborate.

Answer: B

Q9: What was the lady's impression of Henchard based on Elizabeth-Jane's description?

A) That he was a kind and caring father.

B) That he was a hot-tempered and ambitious man.

C) That he was an abusive and cruel parent.

D) That he was indifferent and neglectful towards Elizabeth-Jane.

Answer: B

Q10: What was the main reason Elizabeth-Jane was hesitant about accepting the lady's offer of a position in her household?

A) She felt unqualified and lacked the necessary skills.

B) She was afraid of leaving her father, even though he treated her poorly.

C) She was concerned the lady's motives were not genuine.

D) She did not want to be dependent on someone else.

Answer: A

Q11: Where did the lady inform Elizabeth-Jane she would be living in Casterbridge?

A) In a modest rented cottage.

B) In the Three Mariners inn.

C) In the old stone house called High-Place Hall.

D) With Henchard in his residence.

Answer: C

Q12: How did Elizabeth-Jane's feelings change after the lady's offer of a position in her household?

A) She was hesitant and unsure about accepting.

B) She was excited and relieved at the prospect of leaving her current living situation.

C) She was suspicious and distrustful of the lady's intentions.

D) She was indifferent, as she did not see it as a significant opportunity.

Answer: B

A) She was there to pay her respects to Elizabeth-Jane's late mother.

B) She was searching for clues about Elizabeth-Jane's family history.

C) She was waiting to meet with Elizabeth-Jane by the gravesite.

D) She was comparing the tombstone inscription to something in her guidebook.

17. What was Elizabeth-Jane's reaction to the lady's offer of a position in her household?
   A. She was excited and immediately accepted the offer.
   B. She was hesitant and unsure, believing she was unqualified.
   C. She was suspicious and distrustful of the lady's motives.
   D. She was indifferent, as she did not see it as a significant opportunity.
   **उत्तर:** B

18. How did Henchard's reaction to Elizabeth-Jane's past employment as a waitress differ from Mrs. Stannidge's?
   A. Mrs. Stannidge saw it as a serious offense, while Henchard was indifferent.
   B. Henchard saw it as a social catastrophe, while Mrs. Stannidge attached little importance to it.
   C. Both Henchard and Mrs. Stannidge viewed it as a serious breach of propriety.
   D. Henchard was proud of Elizabeth-Jane's willingness to take on such work.
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

19. What was the significance of the lady's visit to Mrs. Henchard's tombstone in the churchyard?
   A. She was there to pay her respects to Elizabeth-Jane's late mother.
   B. She was searching for clues about Elizabeth-Jane's family history.
   C. She was waiting to meet with Elizabeth-Jane by the gravesite.
   D. She was comparing the tombstone inscription to something in her guidebook.
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

20. What was Henchard's reason for withdrawing his objection to Farfrae courting Elizabeth-Jane?
   A. He genuinely wanted Elizabeth-Jane to be happy.
   B. He hoped it would remove Elizabeth-Jane from his household.
   C. He thought it would bring him a marriage portion from Farfrae.
   D. Both B and C.
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

Q1: What news did Lucetta share with Henchard in her letter?

A) That she had inherited her aunt's fortune

B) That she was getting married to a wealthy man

C) That she had moved to Casterbridge

D) That she had met a new romantic interest

Answer: A

Q2: Why did Lucetta ask Elizabeth-Jane to leave the house for the morning?

A) To go shopping for Lucetta

B) To visit the local museum

C) To give Lucetta time alone with Henchard

D) To avoid upsetting Henchard

Answer: C

A) They were strangers

B) They were friends

C) They were lovers

D) They were married

Answer: C

A) They were close and affectionate

B) They were estranged and had quarreled

C) Henchard was indifferent towards her

D) Henchard was actively hostile towards her

Answer: B

Q5: What was the reason given for High-Place Hall's unpopularity among potential occupants?

A) It was too expensive to maintain

B) It was located in an undesirable part of town

C) It overlooked the market-place, which was considered unsightly

D) It was haunted by a ghostly presence

Answer: C

A) She was from a prestigious family in Bath

B) She was originally from Jersey and had French heritage

C) She was from a military family and had lived in various garrison towns

D) She was from a lower-class background and had recently come into money

Answer: B

A) For Henchard to finally acknowledge Elizabeth-Jane as his daughter

B) For Henchard to formally apologize for his past behavior

C) For Henchard to propose marriage to Lucetta

D) For Henchard to help Lucetta become more accepted in Casterbridge society

Answer: C

A) She did not mention anything about an inheritance

B) She said her aunt had left her a small amount of money

C) She said her aunt had left her a large fortune

D) She said her aunt's death was a tragic event

Answer: C

A) She was still deeply in love with him

B) She was indifferent and wanted to move on

C) She felt guilty and wanted to make amends

D) She was resentful and wanted revenge

Answer: C

A) They greeted each other warmly and cordially

B) They avoided each other and did not speak

C) Henchard was friendly while Farfrae seemed uneasy

D) Farfrae tried to engage Henchard, who rebuffed him

# Set: 25

Q1: Why was Elizabeth-Jane glad to hear Lucetta's words about her staying?

A) Because it meant she would have a home.

B) Because it meant she would have less responsibility.

C) Because it meant she could avoid Henchard.

D) Because it meant she could spend more time with Farfrae.

Answer: A

Q2: What was the main attraction of the carrefour (market-place) for Elizabeth-Jane and Lucetta?

A) It was the center of social activity in the town.

B) It provided a good view of the passing crowds.

C) It was the location of important business transactions.

D) It was the place where they could see Farfrae.

Answer: B

Q3: How often did Elizabeth-Jane and Lucetta visit the market-place?

A) Only on special occasions.

B) Once a week on market day.

C) Twice a week.

D) Whenever they had errands to run.

Answer: B

Q4: What was the new agricultural implement that arrived and caused a sensation?

A) A threshing machine

B) A seed drill

C) A hay baler

D) A reaping machine

Answer: B

Q5: Who did Elizabeth-Jane and Lucetta think was responsible for bringing the new seed drill to the market?

A) Henchard

B) Farfrae

C) A local farmer

D) A traveling salesman

Answer: B

Q6: How did Lucetta describe the appearance of the new seed drill?

A) It resembled a musical instrument.

B) It looked like a giant insect.

C) It was painted in dull, earthy tones.

D) It had a simple, utilitarian design.

Answer: A

Q7: What was Elizabeth-Jane's reaction to the changes the new seed drill would bring to farming practices?

A) She was excited about the technological progress.

B) She was saddened by the loss of the romance of sowing by hand.

C) She was indifferent to the changes.

D) She was skeptical about the drill's effectiveness.

Answer: B

Q8: How did Farfrae respond when Lucetta asked if the seed drill was his?

A) He claimed ownership of the machine.

B) He denied any involvement with it.

C) He said he had merely recommended its purchase.

D) He deferred to Henchard's negative opinion of the machine.

Answer: C

Q9: How did Lucetta and Elizabeth-Jane's interaction with Farfrae at the market affect him?

A) He became more confident and outgoing.

B) He became more reserved and formal.

C) He seemed distracted and focused on Lucetta.

D) He treated them both with equal warmth and ease.

Answer: C

Q10: What did Lucetta observe about Farfrae's shifting moods and demeanor?

A) He was solely focused on his business dealings.

B) He was torn between his mercantile and romantic sides.

C) He was indecisive and unsure of himself.

D) He was consistently polite and deferential.

Answer: B

Q11: How did Henchard react when he encountered Elizabeth-Jane and Lucetta at the market?

A) He greeted them warmly and engaged in friendly conversation.

B) He ignored them and went about his business.

C) He was distant and critical towards Elizabeth-Jane.

D) He expressed displeasure at their presence.

Answer: C

Q12: What did Elizabeth-Jane think she overheard Henchard say to Lucetta?

A) A reproach about her refusing to see him.

B) A compliment on her appearance.

C) An invitation to discuss business.

D) Nothing, it was a hallucination.

Answer: A

B) She acknowledged the incident but refused to discuss it.

C) She became visibly upset and uncomfortable.

D) She dismissed it as Elizabeth-Jane's imagination.

Answer: C

Q14: What did Elizabeth-Jane observe about Lucetta's feelings towards Farfrae?

A) She was indifferent and uninterested in him.

B) She was actively trying to avoid him.

C) She was clearly hoping to encounter him.

D) She was unsure of her own feelings towards him.

Answer: C

Q15: How did Lucetta describe the dilemma faced by the woman in the hypothetical story she told Elizabeth-Jane?

A) She was torn between her duty to her first suitor and her feelings for the second.

B) She was unable to decide which man she loved more.

C) She was trapped in an unwanted marriage to the first man.

D) She was constrained by social conventions from being with the man she preferred.

Answer: A

Q16: What did Lucetta ask Elizabeth-Jane's opinion on regarding the woman's dilemma?

A) Whether she should marry the first man or the second.

B) Whether she should confess her past indiscretion.

C) Whether she should break off the engagement with the first man.

D) Whether she should reveal the full details of her past.

Q17: How did Elizabeth-Jane respond to Lucetta's request for her opinion?

A) She provided a decisive recommendation.

B) She declined to offer an opinion.

C) She suggested they consult a religious authority.

D) She asked Lucetta to provide more details about the situation.

Answer: B

Q18: What did Lucetta express concern about in relation to her own appearance and aging?

B) That she would soon become unattractive to potential suitors.

C) That her beauty would fade too quickly.

D) That she was already past the prime of her looks.

Answer: A

D) She provided a practical estimate of how long Lucetta's looks would last.

Answer: D

Q20: What did Elizabeth-Jane realize about Lucetta's past based on their conversation?

A) That Lucetta had been involved in a scandalous relationship.

B) That Lucetta had been the object of Henchard's affections.

C) That Lucetta was trying to hide something from her.

D) That Lucetta had been compromised in some way.

Answer: D

Q21: What did Lucetta say was the reason for the initial separation between the two people in her hypothetical story?

A) An accident that forced the woman into an equivocal position.

B) The man's lack of education and refinement compared to the second suitor.

C) The woman's discovery of some undesirable qualities in the first man.

D) All of the above.

Answer: D

Q22: What was Elizabeth-Jane's reaction to Lucetta's story about the woman's dilemma?

B) She felt sympathy for the woman's situation.

C) She was intrigued by the ethical complexities involved.

D) She was uncomfortable discussing such a sensitive topic.

Answer: C

Q23: How did Lucetta feel about Elizabeth-Jane's unwillingness to offer an opinion on the woman's dilemma?

C) She was grateful that Elizabeth-Jane remained neutral.

D) She was frustrated by Elizabeth-Jane's reluctance to give advice.

D) Whether she was starting to look old.

Q27: What was the nature of the relationship between Lucetta and the man in her hypothetical story?

Answer: C

C) They were due to circumstances beyond their control.

D) They were the result of mutual misunderstandings.

Answer: C

Q29: How did Lucetta describe the second man the woman in her story had come to prefer?

Q1: What was the position of Elizabeth-Jane in relation to the love triangle between Henchard, Lucetta and Farfrae?

A) She was oblivious to their relationship

B) She was actively involved in their relationship

C) She was an observer and perceived their feelings

D) She was in love with one of them

Answer: C

A) He was confident and assertive

B) He was deferential and awkward

C) He was angry and confrontational

D) He was indifferent and distant

Answer: B

A) She was still in love with Farfrae

B) She wanted to take more time to consider it

C) She was afraid of what others would think

D) She was waiting for a better offer

Answer: B

A) She was indifferent and unmoved

B) She became agitated and upset

C) She became joyful and excited

D) She tried to hide her reaction from Henchard

Answer: C

Q5: What was Elizabeth-Jane's overall perception of the love triangle?

A) She was deeply invested in the outcome

B) She was resentful of the attention Lucetta received

C) She was amused by the absurdity of the situation

D) She was saddened by the neglect she felt from Henchard

Answer: C

A) She was still mourning her late husband

B) She wanted to wait until her inheritance was settled

C) She was not ready to commit to a marriage

D) She was concerned about what people would think

Answer: D

A) He was in awe and admiration of her

B) He felt intimidated and inadequate

C) He was indifferent and unimpressed

D) He was critical and disapproving

Answer: B

A) She was jealous and resentful

B) She was amused and entertained

C) She was saddened and disappointed

D) She was indifferent and unconcerned

Answer: C

# Set: 27

Q1: What was the reason Henchard wanted to meet with the weather prophet known as "Wide-oh"?

A) To learn about the weather forecast for the upcoming harvest

B) To seek a cure for his warts and the "evil"

D) To prove the prophet's forecasts were unreliable

Answer: A

Q2: What was Henchard's reaction when Farfrae expressed sympathy for his recent financial troubles?

A) He gratefully accepted the sympathy

B) He became defensive and tried to downplay the seriousness of his situation

C) He angrily accused Farfrae of mocking him

D) He ignored Farfrae's comments and walked away

Answer: B

Q3: What was the reason Henchard dismissed Jopp from his position?

A) Jopp had stolen from Henchard

B) Jopp had provided poor advice that led to Henchard's financial troubles

C) Jopp had been disloyal and revealed Henchard's secrets

D) Jopp had failed to properly manage Henchard's operations

Answer: B

Q4: How did the weather conditions affect the local farmers and Casterbridge residents at the time?

A) They were indifferent to the weather, as it had little impact on their lives

B) They were deeply affected, seeing the weather as a malevolent force that could ruin them

C) They carefully monitored the weather but remained optimistic regardless of conditions

D) They were only concerned with the weather during the harvest season

Answer: B

Q5: What was the reason Henchard decided to significantly increase his grain purchases despite the poor weather prospects?

B) He was confident in the weather prophet's forecast of an excellent harvest

C) He was attempting to undercut and ruin his rival Farfrae

D) He was speculating on the grain market for financial gain

Answer: D

B) The weather was unfavorable, causing Henchard to incur heavy losses on his grain holdings

D) The weather had no significant impact on Henchard's grain trading activities

Answer: B

B) They were longtime business rivals with a contentious relationship

C) They had a professional but cordial working relationship

D) They had a tumultuous and adversarial relationship

D) He was checking on the status of his accounts and investments

26. What was the reaction of the local community in Casterbridge to the erratic weather conditions and their impact on the grain harvest?
   A. They remained largely indifferent and unaffected by the weather's impact on the harvest
   B. They were deeply concerned and fearful, seeing the weather as a malevolent force that could ruin them
   C. They closely monitored the weather but maintained a calm and optimistic outlook despite the conditions
   D. They were only focused on the weather during the peak of the harvest season, and otherwise paid little attention to it
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

27. How did Henchard's dismissal of Jopp ultimately affect their future relationship and interactions?
   A. Jopp accepted the decision and continued to work for Henchard in a reduced capacity
   B. Jopp became increasingly loyal and dedicated to supporting Henchard's business
   C. Jopp harbored resentment towards Henchard and vowed to find a way to get back at him
   D. Jopp and Henchard were able to reconcile their differences and maintain a professional relationship
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

28. What was the primary reason Henchard decided to significantly increase his grain purchases, despite the poor weather forecasts?
   A. He was attempting to corner the market and drive down prices
   B. He was confident in the weather prophet's prediction of an excellent harvest
   C. He was trying to undercut and ruin his rival Farfrae's grain business
   D. He was speculating on the grain market in hopes of financial gain
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

29. How did Elizabeth-Jane perceive Henchard's hiring of Jopp as his new foreman, given her concerns about Jopp's suitability?
   A. She fully supported the decision and thought Jopp was well-suited for the role
   B. She was indifferent to the hiring, as it had no direct impact on her
   C. She was concerned that Jopp was not the right person for the job
   D. She was unaware of Jopp's hiring, as it was not discussed with her
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

30. What was the relationship between Henchard and the weather prophet "Wide-Oh" at the time of their meeting regarding the weather forecast?
   A. They were long-time acquaintances and had a friendly, trusting relationship
   B. They were meeting for the first time, and Henchard was skeptical of the prophet's abilities
   C. They had a contentious history, with the prophet having previously deceived Henchard
   D. They were relative strangers, but Henchard sought the prophet's expertise on the weather
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

D) Winter

Answer: C

C) A visit from Farfrae

Answer: C

B) Bull Stake

D) High Street

Answer: B

B) To confront Farfrae

D) To check on the progress of the harvest

Answer: C

B) She did not like the town

C) She wanted to travel more

D) She would not be happy there

Answer: D

B) She was afraid he would reveal their past

C) She wanted to make Farfrae jealous

D) Henchard threatened her

Answer: B

B) He offered her money

C) He threatened to reveal their past

D) He said it was the right thing to do

Answer: C

B) She tried to stop Henchard

C) She was indifferent to the situation

D) She was confused by Lucetta's behavior

Answer: B

C) He would reveal their intimacy to others

D) She broke down in tears

A) She loved him deeply

C) She was indifferent to him

D) She was afraid of him

Answer: B

A) He still loved her

B) He wanted to hurt Farfrae

C) He felt it was the right thing to do

D) He wanted her money and status

Answer: C

B) He became angry and confrontational

C) He accepted her decision

B) Release Lucetta from the promise

D) Speak to Farfrae about the situation

Answer: B

A) She welcomed him

B) She was surprised and frightened

C) She was calm and composed

D) She tried to avoid him

Answer: B

B) She took Lucetta's side against Henchard

C) She remained neutral and uninvolved

D) She supported Henchard's demands

Answer: A

B) As a man of good character

C) As a threat to his own interests

D) As someone Lucetta was infatuated with

Answer: A

B) The farmers had sold their crops too early

C) There was a shortage of laborers

D) Henchard had made poor business decisions

Q1: What position did Henchard hold at the time of the events in the passage?

A) Mayor

B) Justice of the Peace

C) Constable

D) Magistrate

Answer: B

Q2: Why was Henchard presiding over the Petty Sessions court that day?

A) He was the Mayor for the year

B) The regular magistrate was absent

C) He was the senior member of the court

D) He was appointed to handle the case

Answer: B

Q3: What was the offense the woman before the court was charged with?

A) Public intoxication

B) Theft

C) Disorderly conduct

D) Verbal assault

Answer: C

Q4: What was the name of the woman charged with the offense?

A) Hannah Dominy

B) Susan Henchard

C) Lucetta Templeman

D) Elizabeth-Jane

Answer: A

Q5: Where did the alleged offense take place according to the constable's testimony?

A) In the town square

B) By the church

C) In the gutter

D) At the furmity tent

Answer: B

Q6: What was the key detail in the constable's testimony that the old woman objected to?

A) The specific location of the offense

B) The time the offense took place

C) The language used by the old woman

D) The constable's identification of the defendant

Answer: A

Q7: What did the old woman claim about her own past and her connection to Henchard?

A) She was a former business partner of Henchard

B) She was an acquaintance of Henchard's wife Susan

C) She had witnessed Henchard selling his wife years ago

D) She had previously provided furmity to Henchard

Answer: C

Q8: How did Henchard react when the old woman revealed this information about him?

A) He dismissed her claims as lies

B) He became angry and defensive

C) He admitted the truth of her story

D) He tried to have the evidence excluded

Answer: C

B) He felt the woman's story proved he was no better than her

C) He wanted to avoid passing judgment on someone like himself

D) He was too disturbed by the revelations to continue presiding

Answer: C

A) They were shocked and disgusted

B) They were amused and entertained

C) They were indifferent to the news

D) They were eager to learn more details

Answer: D

A) That he had abandoned her

B) That he had neglected her

C) That he had sold her at a fair

D) That she had died

Answer: C

Q12: How did Lucetta feel upon learning this new information about Henchard's past?

A) She was relieved

B) She was disgusted

C) She was concerned

D) She was indifferent

Answer: C

A) She decided to end her relationship with him

B) She decided to confront him about it

C) She decided to leave Casterbridge for a time

D) She decided to keep this information private

Answer: C

A) To stay with family

B) To a nearby town

C) To the seaside town of Port-Bredy

D) To visit Elizabeth-Jane

Answer: C

Q15: How did Elizabeth-Jane react to Lucetta's decision to leave Casterbridge?

A) She was concerned for Lucetta's wellbeing

B) She was relieved Lucetta was getting away

C) She was suspicious of Lucetta's motives

D) She was indifferent to Lucetta's plans

Answer: A

A) He was angry and confronted her

## Set: 32

# Set: 32

Q1: What incident had spread through Casterbridge that involved Henchard's past?

A) His dismissal of a worker

B) His bankruptcy proceedings

C) His arrest for public drunkenness

D) His sale of his wife and daughter at a fair

Answer: D

Q2: How did Henchard's fortunes change after the police-court incident was revealed?

A) He continued to prosper and gain respect

B) He immediately lost all of his business dealings

C) He gradually lost esteem and his business declined

D) He was forced to flee the town in disgrace

Answer: C

Q3: What did Henchard do with his gold watch and purse during the bankruptcy proceedings?

A) He hid them away to keep them from his creditors

B) He offered them to his creditors, but they refused to take them

C) He pawned them to try to pay off some of his debts

D) He gave them to Elizabeth-Jane as a final gift

Answer: B

Q4: What was the reaction of the town towards Henchard after his downfall was clear?

A) They continued to condemn him

B) They felt indifferent and unsympathetic

C) They were surprised and regretful about his decline

D) They mocked and ridiculed him for his failures

Answer: C

Q5: Why did Elizabeth-Jane want to be allowed to see and forgive Henchard?

A) She felt he had treated her unfairly in the past

B) She wanted to gloat over his downfall and humiliation

C) She still believed in him and wanted to help him

D) She was curious to see the once-prosperous man in ruin

Answer: C

Q6: Where had Henchard moved to after leaving his former prosperous home?

A) To another town entirely to start over

B) Into a small cottage by the old Priory Mill

C) Into the home of one of his former creditors

D) Into the workhouse, having lost everything

Answer: B

Q7: What change had occurred at Henchard's former business headquarters?

A) It had been abandoned and fallen into disrepair

B) It was being run by Jopp, Henchard's former employee

C) Farfrae had taken over the business and operations

D) It had been taken over by the local authorities

Answer: C

Q8: What did Abel Whittle say had improved for the workers under Farfrae's management?

A) Wages were higher and working conditions better

B) There was less fear and stress in their work

C) The hours were shorter and the work less demanding

D) Henchard's harsh treatment of them had ceased

Answer: B

Q9: How long had the incident at Weydon-Priors Fair that involved Henchard been known in Casterbridge before the furmity-woman's retort?

A) It had been common knowledge for years

B) It had only recently been revealed

C) It had been a long-buried secret until then

D) It was still not widely known until the retort

Answer: C

Q10: What caused Henchard's credit and business to become unstable, beyond the police-court incident?

A) Rash business dealings and a bad harvest year

B) Embezzlement by one of his employees

C) The failure of a debtor he had trusted

D) All of the above

Answer: D

Q11: What did the senior Commissioner say about Henchard's behavior during the bankruptcy proceedings?

A) That Henchard had been dishonest and tried to hide assets

B) That Henchard had been as fair and transparent as possible

C) That Henchard had been uncooperative and obstructive

D) That Henchard had shown no remorse for his actions

Answer: B

Q12: What did Henchard do with the money from selling his gold watch?

A) He used it to pay off one of his smaller creditors

B) He kept it for himself, unwilling to part with his last possessions

C) He donated it anonymously to a local charity

D) He gave it to Elizabeth-Jane as a final gesture

Answer: A

Q13: What was Henchard's reaction when the creditors refused to take his watch and money?

A) He was relieved and grateful they let him keep them

B) He was offended and threw the items down in disgust

C) He was confused and unsure of what to do with them

D) He was suspicious that they were trying to trick him

Answer: C

Q14: What metaphor was used to describe Henchard's downfall in fortunes and esteem?

A) He had reached the pinnacle and then plummeted

B) He had climbed to great heights only to crash and burn

C) He had passed the ridge and began a rapid descent

D) He had soared high only to have his wings clipped

Answer: C

Q15: How did Elizabeth-Jane try to reach out to Henchard after his downfall?

A) She confronted him angrily about his past actions

B) She visited his home and demanded to see him

C) She wrote to him, hoping he would reply

D) She enlisted the help of the local authorities

Answer: C

Q16: What detail was noted about the cottage Henchard had moved into?

A) It was a newly built and modern dwelling

B) It was dilapidated and constructed from old Priory ruins

C) It was cozy and comfortable, a peaceful retreat

D) It was located in a remote and isolated area

Answer: B

Q17: What had been done to obscure Henchard's name on the signage at his former business?

A) It had been covered over with a plain paint color

B) It had been roughly scratched out and defaced

C) It had been carefully removed and replaced

D) Farfrae's name had been superimposed over it

Answer: A

Q18: Why did Abel Whittle say the workers were better off under Farfrae's management?

A) There was less fear and stress, even with lower wages

B) The work was less demanding, with shorter hours

C) Farfrae was more approachable and less temperamental

D) All of the above

Answer: C

Q19: How did Henchard's financial situation and business dealings contribute to his downfall?

A) He made overly risky and speculative transactions

B) He trusted debtors who ultimately failed to pay him

C) One of his own employees mishandled a large grain shipment

D) All of the above

Answer: D
