## Set: 33

# Set: 33

Q1: What was the distinction between the two bridges in Casterbridge mentioned in the passage?

A) One was made of brick and the other of stone

B) One was located in the town center and the other in the meadows

C) One was used by the more respectable people while the other by the failures

D) All of the above

Answer: D

Q2: What did the passage say was the common reason why "desperate men" would wrench off the coping of the bridges?

A) To use it as building material

B) To throw it into the river in defiance

C) To sell it for scrap metal

D) To prevent others from using the bridges

Answer: B

Q3: Which type of people were more likely to be found brooding on the remote stone bridge?

A) Those who had failed in business, love, or morality

B) The unemployed and socially disgraced professionals

C) The poor and destitute of the town

D) None

Answer: B

Q4: How did the passage describe the difference in demeanor between those on the two bridges?

A) Those on the town bridge were more open and less self-conscious

B) Those on the remote bridge were more detached and avoided eye contact

C) Those on the town bridge were more aggressive and confrontational

D) There was no notable difference in their behavior

Answer: B

Q5: What did the passage suggest might happen to some who brooded too long on the remote bridge?

A) They would eventually become numb to their troubles

B) They would seek help and counseling

C) They would jump off the bridge into the river

D) They would abandon the bridge and move to a new town

Answer: C

Q6: Who did Henchard encounter when he was standing on the remote stone bridge?

A) Lucetta, his former paramour

B) Elizabeth-Jane, his estranged stepdaughter

C) Farfrae, his former business rival

D) Jopp, his former foreman

Answer: D

Q7: What news did Jopp share with Henchard about Farfrae taking possession of Henchard's former home?

A) Farfrae had purchased the house and all of Henchard's furniture

B) Farfrae was renting the house and had Henchard's furniture moved out

C) Farfrae had evicted Henchard from the house and sold his belongings

D) Farfrae was allowing Henchard to continue living in the house

Answer: A

Q8: How did Henchard initially react to the news of Farfrae living in his former home?

A) He was indifferent and unbothered by it

B) He was deeply hurt and angered by it

C) He was surprised but unsure how to feel

D) He felt resigned and accepted the situation

Answer: B

Q9: What offer did Farfrae make to Henchard when they encountered each other on the bridge?

A) To give Henchard his old job back

B) To let Henchard live rent-free in his former home

C) To help Henchard emigrate to start a new life

D) To buy Henchard's remaining possessions

Answer: B

Q10: Why did Henchard refuse Farfrae's offer to let him live in his old house?

A) He was too proud to accept charity from his rival

B) He felt it would lead to constant quarreling between them

C) He was determined to start a new life elsewhere

D) He did not want to be reminded of his former prosperity

Answer: B

Q11: What did Farfrae offer to allow Henchard to take back from the house?

A) Any personal belongings or furnishings he wanted

B) The deed to the house so he could reclaim ownership

C) A portion of the money Farfrae paid for the house

D) The lease on the property so Henchard could live there

Answer: A

Q12: How did Henchard react when Farfrae made this offer regarding the household items?

A) He was touched and grateful for the gesture

B) He became angry and refused to accept anything

C) He was suspicious that it was a trick or trap

D) He tried to negotiate for more possessions

Answer: A

Q13: Where was Elizabeth-Jane living at this point, and what was she observing from her window?

A) She was living in a small cottage, watching Farfrae and Lucetta in their new home

B) She was living in her old room in Henchard's former house, watching the comings and goings

C) She was living in a boarding house, observing Henchard's interactions with Farfrae

D) She was living in the attic of the Prophet's house, watching Henchard's activities

Answer: A

Q14: How did Elizabeth-Jane react when she heard the news that Henchard had "busted out drinking" after 21 years of abstinence?

A) She was surprised and saddened by the news

B) She went to confront Henchard about his relapse

C) She rushed out to try and help and support him

D) She resigned herself to not being able to intervene

Answer: C

Q15: What had Henchard sworn an oath to do 21 years prior that he was now intending to break?

A) To never remarry after losing his wife

B) To abstain from drinking alcoholic beverages

C) To leave Casterbridge and never return

D) To give away all his wealth and possessions

Answer: B

Q16: How did Henchard describe his impending "release" from this 21-year oath to his co-worker Solomon Longways?

A) As a joyful liberation after so many years of deprivation

B) As a necessary step to regain his former status and prosperity

C) As a way to dull the pain of his current misfortunes

D) As a reckless and defiant act of rebellion

Answer: D

Q17: What position was Farfrae rumored to be proposed for in the Casterbridge town council in the near future?

A) Councilman

B) Sheriff

C) Town Clerk

D) Mayor

Answer: D

Q18: How did Henchard view Farfrae's likely election as Mayor, given their history?

A) He was proud that his former protégé had risen so far

B) He was indifferent and felt Farfrae deserved the position

C) He was resentful and saw it as Farfrae's ultimate triumph

D) He was hopeful that it would lead to reconciliation between them

Answer: C

Q19: What specific job did Henchard take on working for Farfrae after losing his own business?

A) Clerk in Farfrae's offices

B) Laborer in Farfrae's warehouses

C) Hay-trusser in Farfrae's farms

D) Driver of Farfrae's delivery wagons

Answer: C

Q20: How was Henchard's appearance and attire different now as a day laborer compared to his former prosperous days?

A) He still dressed respectably in clean, suitable clothes

B) He wore the same fine clothes but they were now worn and shabby

C) He wore simple workman's garb, a stark contrast to before

D) He refused to wear any proper clothing, dressing in rags

Answer: C

Q21: How did Elizabeth-Jane learn of Henchard's relapse into drinking after 21 years of abstinence?

A) She overheard a conversation about it in the street below her window

B) Henchard himself confessed it to her during one of her visits

C) Farfrae informed her of Henchard's concerning behavior

D) The news spread through the town and eventually reached her

Answer: A

Q22: Why did Farfrae have his orders and interactions with Henchard done through a foreman rather than directly?

A) To maintain a formal, professional distance

B) To avoid upsetting or angering the volatile Henchard

C) To make it clear Henchard was now a subordinate employee

D) All of the above

Answer: D

Q23: What specific detail was noted about the condition of the two bridges in Casterbridge?

B) They were adorned with decorative metalwork

C) They showed signs of heavy use over many years

D) They were in a state of disrepair and neglect

Answer: C

Q24: How did the passage describe the contrast between Henchard's prosperous past and his current status as a day laborer?

D) It showed Henchard's resilience in the face of adversity

C) She had moved back into Henchard's former house

D) She had taken a position as a live-in servant

Q28: How did Farfrae's approach to managing and interacting with Henchard differ from Henchard's own previous style as a boss?

   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

3. Which type of people were more likely to be found brooding on the remote stone bridge?
   A. Those who had failed in business, love, or morality
   B. The unemployed and socially disgraced professionals
   C. The poor and destitute of the town
   D. None
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

4. How did the passage describe the difference in demeanor between those on the two bridges?
   A. Those on the town bridge were more open and less self-conscious
   B. Those on the remote bridge were more detached and avoided eye contact
   C. Those on the town bridge were more aggressive and confrontational
   D. There was no notable difference in their behavior
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

5. What did the passage suggest might happen to some who brooded too long on the remote bridge?
   A. They would eventually become numb to their troubles
   B. They would seek help and counseling
   C. They would jump off the bridge into the river
   D. They would abandon the bridge and move to a new town
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

6. Who did Henchard encounter when he was standing on the remote stone bridge?
   A. Lucetta, his former paramour
   B. Elizabeth-Jane, his estranged stepdaughter
   C. Farfrae, his former business rival
   D. Jopp, his former foreman
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

7. What news did Jopp share with Henchard about Farfrae taking possession of Henchard's former home?
   A. Farfrae had purchased the house and all of Henchard's furniture
   B. Farfrae was renting the house and had Henchard's furniture moved out
   C. Farfrae had evicted Henchard from the house and sold his belongings
   D. Farfrae was allowing Henchard to continue living in the house
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

8. How did Henchard initially react to the news of Farfrae living in his former home?
   A. He was indifferent and unbothered by it
   B. He was deeply hurt and angered by it
   C. He was surprised but unsure how to feel
   D. He felt resigned and accepted the situation
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

9. What offer did Farfrae make to Henchard when they encountered each other on the bridge?
   A. To give Henchard his old job back
   B. To let Henchard live rent-free in his former home
   C. To help Henchard emigrate to start a new life
   D. To buy Henchard's remaining possessions
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

10. Why did Henchard refuse Farfrae's offer to let him live in his old house?
   A. He was too proud to accept charity from his rival
   B. He felt it would lead to constant quarreling between them
   C. He was determined to start a new life elsewhere
   D. He did not want to be reminded of his former prosperity
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

11. What did Farfrae offer to allow Henchard to take back from the house?
   A. Any personal belongings or furnishings he wanted
   B. The deed to the house so he could reclaim ownership
   C. A portion of the money Farfrae paid for the house
   D. The lease on the property so Henchard could live there
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

12. How did Henchard react when Farfrae made this offer regarding the household items?
   A. He was touched and grateful for the gesture
   B. He became angry and refused to accept anything
   C. He was suspicious that it was a trick or trap
   D. He tried to negotiate for more possessions
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

13. Where was Elizabeth-Jane living at this point, and what was she observing from her window?
   A. She was living in a small cottage, watching Farfrae and Lucetta in their new home
   B. She was living in her old room in Henchard's former house, watching the comings and goings
   C. She was living in a boarding house, observing Henchard's interactions with Farfrae
   D. She was living in the attic of the Prophet's house, watching Henchard's activities
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

14. How did Elizabeth-Jane react when she heard the news that Henchard had "busted out drinking" after 21 years of abstinence?
   A. She was surprised and saddened by the news
   B. She went to confront Henchard about his relapse
   C. She rushed out to try and help and support him
   D. She resigned herself to not being able to intervene
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

15. What had Henchard sworn an oath to do 21 years prior that he was now intending to break?
   A. To never remarry after losing his wife
   B. To abstain from drinking alcoholic beverages
   C. To leave Casterbridge and never return
   D. To give away all his wealth and possessions
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

16. How did Henchard describe his impending "release" from this 21-year oath to his co-worker Solomon Longways?
   A. As a joyful liberation after so many years of deprivation
   B. As a necessary step to regain his former status and prosperity
   C. As a way to dull the pain of his current misfortunes
   D. As a reckless and defiant act of rebellion
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

17. What position was Farfrae rumored to be proposed for in the Casterbridge town council in the near future?
   A. Councilman
   B. Sheriff
   C. Town Clerk
   D. Mayor
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

18. How did Henchard view Farfrae's likely election as Mayor, given their history?
   A. He was proud that his former protégé had risen so far
   B. He was indifferent and felt Farfrae deserved the position
   C. He was resentful and saw it as Farfrae's ultimate triumph
   D. He was hopeful that it would lead to reconciliation between them
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

19. What specific job did Henchard take on working for Farfrae after losing his own business?
   A. Clerk in Farfrae's offices
   B. Laborer in Farfrae's warehouses
   C. Hay-trusser in Farfrae's farms
   D. Driver of Farfrae's delivery wagons
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

20. How was Henchard's appearance and attire different now as a day laborer compared to his former prosperous days?
   A. He still dressed respectably in clean, suitable clothes
   B. He wore the same fine clothes but they were now worn and shabby
   C. He wore simple workman's garb, a stark contrast to before
   D. He refused to wear any proper clothing, dressing in rags
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

21. How did Elizabeth-Jane learn of Henchard's relapse into drinking after 21 years of abstinence?
   A. She overheard a conversation about it in the street below her window
   B. Henchard himself confessed it to her during one of her visits
   C. Farfrae informed her of Henchard's concerning behavior
   D. The news spread through the town and eventually reached her
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

22. Why did Farfrae have his orders and interactions with Henchard done through a foreman rather than directly?
   A. To maintain a formal, professional distance
   B. To avoid upsetting or angering the volatile Henchard
   C. To make it clear Henchard was now a subordinate employee
   D. All of the above
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

23. What specific detail was noted about the condition of the two bridges in Casterbridge?
   A. They were frequently vandalized and damaged
   B. They were adorned with decorative metalwork
   C. They showed signs of heavy use over many years
   D. They were in a state of disrepair and neglect
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

24. How did the passage describe the contrast between Henchard's prosperous past and his current status as a day laborer?
   A. It highlighted the dramatic reversal of their fortunes
   B. It emphasized Henchard's stubborn refusal to give up
   C. It depicted Henchard as a symbol of social mobility
   D. It showed Henchard's resilience in the face of adversity
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

25. What type of work was Henchard specifically tasked with performing for Farfrae as a hired laborer?
   A. Clerical and administrative duties
   B. Manual labor in the fields and granaries
   C. Driving and delivery of goods
   D. Maintenance and repairs around the property
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

26. What was the specific detail about the duration of Henchard's oath of abstinence that he was about to break?
   A. It had been 20 years since he took the oath
   B. It was 21 years to the day that he had sworn it
   C. He had originally taken the oath for a lifetime
   D. There was no specific duration mentioned
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

27. What was Elizabeth-Jane's living situation like at this point, compared to her previous more affluent circumstances?
   A. She was living in a small, modest room in town
   B. She was staying with relatives in the countryside
   C. She had moved back into Henchard's former house
   D. She had taken a position as a live-in servant
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

28. How did Farfrae's approach to managing and interacting with Henchard differ from Henchard's own previous style as a boss?
   A. Farfrae was more formal and detached
   B. Farfrae was more confrontational and critical
   C. Farfrae was more hands-on and involved
   D. Farfrae treated Henchard as an equal
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

29. What was the specific location where Henchard encountered Jopp and received the news about Farfrae taking over his former home?
   A. On the brick bridge in the town center
   B. On the stone bridge in the outlying meadows
   C. At the entrance to Henchard's former property
   D. Inside the house that Farfrae was now occupying
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

30. What was the general public perception of Henchard's downfall and reversal of fortunes described in the passage?
   A. They continued to strongly condemn him
   B. They were largely indifferent and unsympathetic
   C. They were surprised but regretful about his decline
   D. They mocked and ridiculed him for his failures
   **उत्तर:** C

## Set: 34

# Set: 34

Q1: What was the convivial custom that prevailed in Casterbridge on Sunday afternoons?

A) The churchgoers would gather at the town square for a civic ceremony.

B) The choir members would gather at the Three Mariners Inn for drinks.

C) The townspeople would hold a picnic in the local park.

D) The men of the congregation would file from the church doors to the Three Mariners Inn.

Answer: D

Q2: What was the point of honor for the men who gathered at the Three Mariners Inn?

A) To drink as much as they could.

B) To discuss the sermon in depth.

C) To strictly limit themselves to half-a-pint of liquor.

D) To sing hymns and psalms.

Answer: C

Q3: What did Henchard do to ensure that the choir sang the Hundred-and-Ninth Psalm to the tune of Wiltshire?

A) He bribed the choir members.

B) He threatened them with bodily harm.

C) He joined the choir and led them in the performance.

D) He persuaded them with his eloquent oratory.

Answer: B

Q4: What was Henchard's response when the choir members expressed reluctance to sing the Hundred-and-Ninth Psalm?

A) He apologized and let them sing a different psalm.

B) He became enraged and barricaded the door to prevent their departure.

C) He joined in the singing and led the choir.

D) He dismissed the choir and sang the psalm himself.

Answer: B

Q5: What was Henchard's reaction after the choir had finished singing the Hundred-and-Ninth Psalm?

A) He was indifferent and left the room.

B) He was deeply moved and expressed his appreciation.

C) He criticized the choir's performance and demanded they sing it again.

D) He became angry and threatened the choir members.

Answer: B

Q6: What was Henchard's attitude towards Farfrae and Lucetta when he saw them passing by?

A) He was indifferent and ignored them.

B) He expressed happiness at their presence.

C) He made sarcastic comments about the psalm they had just sung.

D) He threatened to harm them.

Answer: C

Q7: What was Elizabeth-Jane's reaction when she heard Henchard's threats against Farfrae?

A) She encouraged Henchard to confront Farfrae.

B) She tried to calm Henchard down and persuade him not to act.

C) She reported Henchard's threats to the authorities.

D) She did nothing, as she was not concerned about the situation.

Answer: B

Q8: How did Lucetta respond when she encountered Henchard in the yard?

A) She ignored him and continued on her way.

B) She greeted him warmly and engaged in conversation.

C) She expressed embarrassment and discomfort at the encounter.

D) She confronted him about his sarcastic remarks.

Answer: C

Q9: What was Lucetta's response to Henchard's sarcastic comments during their encounter in the yard?

A) She did not respond and continued on her way.

B) She confronted Henchard and demanded he treat her with respect.

C) She wrote a note to Henchard, asking him to refrain from such behavior.

D) She reported Henchard's behavior to her husband, Farfrae.

Answer: C

Q10: How did Henchard react to Lucetta's note requesting him to refrain from sarcastic comments?

A) He complied with her request and treated her respectfully thereafter.

B) He became more aggressive and vindictive towards her.

C) He tore up the note and burned it, dismissing her request.

D) He showed the note to Farfrae, hoping to cause trouble between the couple.

Answer: C

Q11: What was Elizabeth-Jane's purpose for going to the yard where Henchard and Whittle were working?

A) To observe the interaction between Henchard and Farfrae.

B) To ask Henchard to join her for tea.

C) To replace Whittle and work alongside Henchard herself.

D) To check on Henchard's well-being and ensure he was not causing trouble.

Answer: A

Q12: What did Elizabeth-Jane witness when she observed Henchard and Farfrae in the corn-stores?

A) Henchard embracing Farfrae in a friendly gesture.

B) Henchard raising his hand behind Farfrae, in a potentially threatening manner.

C) Farfrae giving instructions to Henchard about his work duties.

D) Nothing out of the ordinary, as the two men were simply conversing.

Answer: B

Q13: What was Elizabeth-Jane's reaction to what she observed between Henchard and Farfrae in the corn-stores?

A) She felt relieved and reassured that there was no threat.

B) She became angry at Henchard's actions and confronted him.

C) She was deeply concerned and resolved to warn Farfrae about Henchard's behavior.

D) She dismissed the incident as her imagination and did not think it worthy of concern.

Answer: C

Q14: How did the choir members initially respond to Henchard's request to sing the Hundred-and-Ninth Psalm?

A) They enthusiastically agreed to sing the psalm.

B) They refused to sing the psalm, citing it as inappropriate.

C) They hesitated and tried to persuade Henchard to choose a different psalm.

D) They informed Henchard that they did not know the Hundred-and-Ninth Psalm.

Answer: C

Q15: What was the reason given by the choir leader for not wanting to sing the Hundred-and-Ninth Psalm?

A) The lyrics were too gloomy and depressing.

B) The psalm was not suitable for Sunday worship.

C) The tune was too difficult for the choir to perform.

D) The lyrics were inappropriate and could be seen as directed at a specific person.

Answer: D

Q16: What was the reaction of the choir members when Henchard insisted they sing the Hundred-and-Ninth Psalm?

A) They complied without further protest.

B) They continued to refuse and left the room.

C) They argued with Henchard and tried to convince him to choose a different psalm.

D) They sang the psalm, but with visible discomfort and reluctance.

Answer: D

Q17: What was Henchard's reason for wanting the choir to sing the Hundred-and-Ninth Psalm?

A) He believed the lyrics were particularly meaningful and appropriate for the occasion.

B) He wanted to use the psalm as a means of expressing his anger towards Farfrae.

C) He enjoyed the particular tune and felt it would lift his spirits.

D) He was simply in a defiant mood and wanted to assert his authority over the choir.

Answer: B

Q18: What was Henchard's emotional state after the choir had finished singing the Hundred-and-Ninth Psalm?

A) He was indifferent and unaffected by the performance.

B) He was deeply moved and expressed his appreciation to the choir.

C) He remained angry and dissatisfied, demanding that they sing the psalm again.

D) He became more agitated and confrontational towards the choir members.

Answer: B

Q19: How did Henchard's attitude towards Farfrae and Lucetta change after the encounter in the yard?

A) He became more cordial and friendly towards them.

B) He continued to treat them with the same level of indifference as before.

C) He became increasingly hostile and resentful towards them.

D) He avoided any further interaction with them.

Answer: C

Q20: What was Elizabeth-Jane's concern about Henchard's behavior towards Farfrae?

A) She was worried that Henchard might physically harm Farfrae.

B) She was concerned that Henchard's sarcasm and resentment might escalate.

C) She feared that Henchard's actions could jeopardize Farfrae's business.

D) All of the above.

Answer: D

Q21: What was the reason Lucetta gave for requesting Henchard to refrain from his sarcastic comments towards her?

A) She claimed that his behavior was causing her emotional distress.

B) She stated that she bore him no ill-will and only wanted to be treated respectfully.

C) She threatened to report his behavior to her husband, Farfrae.

D) Both A and B.

Answer: D

Q22: How did Henchard respond to Lucetta's written request to him?

A) He complied with her request and treated her more respectfully going forward.

B) He tore up the note and burned it, dismissing her concerns.

C) He showed the note to Farfrae, hoping to cause trouble between the couple.

D) He confronted Lucetta in person and expressed his frustration with her.

Answer: B

Q23: What was the purpose of Elizabeth-Jane's visits to bring tea to Henchard in the evenings?

A) To ensure Henchard did not drink too much at the Three Mariners Inn.

B) To have a chance to observe Henchard's interactions with Farfrae.

C) To provide Henchard with companionship and comfort during his difficult circumstances.

D) Both A and B.

Answer: D

Q24: What was Henchard's reaction when Elizabeth-Jane brought him tea in the corn-stores?

A) He was grateful and accepted the tea gratefully.

B) He refused the tea and sent Elizabeth-Jane away.

C) He ignored the tea and continued his work.

D) He used the opportunity to discuss his concerns about Farfrae with Elizabeth-Jane.

Answer: C

Q25: What was the reason Henchard gave for not wanting to work alongside Abel Whittle in the yard?

A) He disliked Whittle's personality and found him irritating.

B) He felt that Whittle's pitying gaze was a constant reminder of his fallen status.

C) He wanted to avoid any interaction with Farfrae's employees.

D) He preferred to work alone and found Whittle's presence distracting.

Answer: B

Q26: What was Elizabeth-Jane's suggestion to Henchard regarding working in the yard?

A) She offered to take Whittle's place and work alongside Henchard herself.

B) She advised Henchard to try to ignore Whittle's presence and focus on his own work.

C) She recommended that Henchard request a different work assignment to avoid Whittle.

D) She did not make any suggestions, as she did not want to interfere in Henchard's work situation.

Answer: A

Q27: How did Henchard's drinking habits change after his encounter with Farfrae and Lucetta in the yard?

A) He stopped drinking altogether, as he was determined to maintain his sobriety.

B) He continued to drink at his usual pace, without any noticeable change.

C) He began drinking more heavily at the Three Mariners Inn every evening.

D) He limited his drinking to only special occasions, such as Sundays.

Answer: C

Q28: What was Lucetta's reaction when she unexpectedly came across Henchard in the yard?

A) She greeted him warmly and engaged in friendly conversation.

B) She expressed discomfort and quickly left the area to avoid further interaction.

C) She confronted Henchard about his sarcastic remarks and demanded he treat her with respect.

D) She ignored Henchard and continued on her way, pretending not to notice him.

Answer: B

Q29: What was Henchard's attitude towards the choir's reluctance to sing the Hundred-and-Ninth Psalm?

A) He understood their concerns and agreed to choose a different psalm.

B) He became increasingly angry and threatened the choir members.

C) He dismissed their objections and insisted they sing the psalm regardless.

D) He tried to persuade the choir to sing the psalm by appealing to their sense of duty.

Answer: C

Q30: How did Henchard's relationship with Farfrae and Lucetta change after the incident in the yard?

A) Henchard became more cordial and accepting of their presence.

B) Henchard continued to treat them with the same level of indifference as before.

C) Henchard's resentment and hostility towards them grew more pronounced.

D) Henchard avoided any further interaction with Farfrae and Lucetta.

Answer: C

Q1: Why did Elizabeth-Jane go to meet Farfrae so early in the morning?

A) To invite him to have breakfast with her and Henchard.

B) To warn Farfrae about Henchard's potential hostility towards him.

C) To ask Farfrae for a job in his business.

D) To congratulate Farfrae on his recent election as mayor.

Answer: B

A) He was dismissive and brushed off her concerns.

B) He became angry and threatened to confront Henchard.

C) He thanked Elizabeth-Jane for the warning and said he would be cautious.

D) He assured Elizabeth-Jane that he and Henchard were good friends and there was no need for concern.

Answer: D

B) He expressed bitterness towards Henchard and vowed to get revenge.

C) He was surprised and saddened, but said he would continue to employ Henchard.

D) He denied the town-clerk's claims, insisting that Henchard could not harbor any ill will towards him.

Answer: C

D) He consulted with Lucetta first before providing a response.

Answer: B

D) She suggested that Farfrae confront Henchard directly to resolve the issue.

Answer: B

Q1: Why did Lucetta retire to her room early?

B) The sound of someone cooking dinner

C) The sound of someone cleaning the room

D) The sound of someone watching television

Answer: A

B) Her secret financial dealings

C) Her plan to leave her husband

D) Her affair with another man

Answer: A

A) To confront him directly

B) To have her husband confront him

C) To persuade him not to reveal her secret

D) To have him arrested

Answer: C

A) To avoid being recognized

B) To appear more sympathetic

C) To match Henchard's own plain attire

D) To show her humility

Answer: B

B) He was indifferent and cynical

C) He was compassionate and sympathetic

D) He was nervous and uncertain

Answer: C

B) Destroy the letters he had been reading

C) Help her leave her husband

D) Return the letters and papers he had regarding her

Answer: D

Q8: What was Lucetta's main concern about her husband finding out her secret?

A) That he would no longer love her

B) That he would divorce her

D) That he would harm her physically

   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

13. What did Henchard call his reading of the letters to Farfrae?
   A. A public confession
   B. An exposé of Lucetta's past
   C. A practical joke
   D. An act of revenge
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

14. What did Henchard say he would do with the letters and papers?
   A. Publish them in the local newspaper
   B. Show them to Lucetta's husband
   C. Keep them as leverage against her
   D. Return them to Lucetta without fail
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

15. What did Lucetta hope would happen if her husband found out her secret later on?
   A. That he would divorce her immediately
   B. That he would be understanding and forgive her
   C. That he would never find out the truth
   D. That he would confront Henchard about it
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

16. What was Lucetta's main concern about revealing her secret to her husband?
   A. That he would no longer trust her
   B. That he would stop loving her
   C. That he would believe it was her fault
   D. All of the above
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

17. How did Lucetta describe Henchard's threat to reveal her secret?
   A. As an act of revenge
   B. As a cruel betrayal
   C. As a "horse-play"
   D. As a necessary truth
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

18. What did Lucetta say had pulled her down and made her look ill?
   A. Her poor health and lack of sleep
   B. The demands of being a wife and homemaker
   C. All of Henchard's actions and threats
   D. The stress of her past relationship with Henchard
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

19. What did Henchard say he had only meant his reading of the letters to be?
   A. A genuine attempt to confess his own wrongdoing
   B. A way to prove Lucetta's innocence
   C. A harmless practical joke
   D. An act of revenge against Lucetta
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

20. What did Lucetta hope Henchard would do after seeing her vulnerable state?
   A. Expose her secret to her husband
   B. Blackmail her for more money
   C. Pity her and refrain from further harm
   D. Leave her alone and never contact her again
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

21. Why did Lucetta's choice of meeting location affect Henchard's reaction?
   A. It reminded him of his past relationship with another woman
   B. It made him feel more confident and in control of the situation
   C. It made him uncomfortable and unsure of how to proceed
   D. It angered him and made him more determined to reveal her secret
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

22. What did Henchard say he would do to ensure Lucetta's secret remained hidden?
   A. Destroy the letters and papers
   B. Promise to never reveal the information
   C. Confront her husband directly
   D. Both A and B
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

23. Why did Lucetta's "want of foresight" regarding the meeting place surprise Henchard?
   A. Because it made her seem more vulnerable and naive
   B. Because it showed she was not as calculating as he had assumed
   C. Because it reminded him of his own past mistakes
   D. All of the above
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

24. How did Henchard describe Lucetta's current situation to her?
   A. As a well-deserved comeuppance
   B. As a temporary setback she could overcome
   C. As a tragic result of her past actions
   D. As an unfair burden she was unfairly forced to bear
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

25. What did Lucetta hope Henchard's pity and understanding would lead him to do?
   A. Reveal her secret to her husband anyway
   B. Help her leave her husband
   C. Agree to return her letters and papers
   D. Arrange for her husband to never find out the truth
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

26. What did Lucetta's "poor, plainest and longest discarded attire" suggest about her emotional state?
   A. That she was trying to appear more humble and penitent
   B. That she had neglected her appearance due to stress and fatigue
   C. That she was intentionally trying to appear unattractive
   D. Both B and C
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

27. How did Lucetta describe the threat of Henchard revealing her secret to her husband?
   A. As an act of cruelty and betrayal
   B. As a "horse-play" that was merely a practical joke
   C. As a necessary truth that her husband deserved to know
   D. As a justified punishment for her past actions
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

28. What did Henchard say he would do if Lucetta's husband found out her secret eventually?
   A. He would deny any involvement
   B. He would support Lucetta and defend her
   C. He told her he was sure her husband would find out eventually
   D. Both A and C
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

29. What was Lucetta's main concern about her husband discovering her secret?
   A. That he would no longer see her as virtuous and deserving
   B. That he would immediately divorce her
   C. That he would physically harm her
   D. That he would lose all respect for her
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

30. How did Henchard's past experiences with another wronged woman affect his reaction to Lucetta?
   A. It made him more sympathetic and unwilling to further harm her
   B. It angered him and made him more determined to expose her secret
   C. It had no effect, as he was only concerned with his own interests
   D. It caused him to become confused and unsure of how to proceed
   **उत्तर:** A

## Set: 37

# Set: 37

Q1: Who came to speak to Lucetta as she was returning home?

A) Her husband Donald Farfrae

B) Henchard

C) Jopp

D) A local corn-merchant

Answer: C

Q2: What did Jopp ask Lucetta to do for him?

A) Recommend him to her husband for a working partnership

B) Give him money to start a new business

C) Reveal Henchard's secret to her husband

D) Intercede on his behalf with the local authorities

Answer: A

Q3: How did Lucetta respond to Jopp's request?

A) She eagerly agreed to help him

B) She promised to speak to her husband on his behalf

C) She coldly refused to get involved

D) She threatened to have him arrested

Answer: C

Q4: What did Henchard ask Jopp to do for him?

A) Deliver a package to Lucetta

B) Find a new job for him

C) Speak to Lucetta on his behalf

D) Destroy a set of letters

Answer: A

Q5: What did Jopp do after receiving the package from Henchard?

A) Delivered it to Lucetta as requested

B) Opened the package and read the contents

C) Took it to the local authorities

D) Burned the contents of the package

Answer: B

Q6: Where did Jopp encounter Mother Cuxsom and Nance Mockridge?

A) At the Three Mariners Inn

B) On the bridge over the river

C) In Mixen Lane

D) At Lucetta's home

Answer: C

Q7: What did Jopp say was inside the package he was carrying?

A) Official documents

B) Henchard's personal belongings

C) Love letters written by Lucetta

D) A report on criminal activities in the town

Answer: C

Q8: What did the women in Mixen Lane discuss doing with the information about Lucetta's love letters?

A) Using it to blackmail Lucetta

B) Publishing the letters in the local newspaper

C) Staging a "skimmity-ride" to publicly shame her

D) Turning the letters over to the authorities

Answer: C

Q9: What did the landlady say the "skimmity-ride" custom was?

A) A celebration of infidelity in the town

B) A punishment for unfaithful wives

C) A way for the community to mock and shame a disgraced person

D) An old-fashioned festival with music and dancing

Answer: C

Q10: Who was the stranger who came across the stream and entered the inn?

A) A local poacher

B) A traveling salesman

C) An investigator looking into the town's affairs

D) An eccentric gentleman passing through

Answer: D

Q11: What did the stranger offer to contribute towards the planned "skimmity-ride"?

A) A substantial sum of money

B) His help in organizing the event

C) Supplies and refreshments for the participants

D) His promise to keep the event a secret

Answer: A

Q12: How did the landlady describe the "skimmity-ride" custom to the stranger?

A) As an ancient ritual to cleanse the town of sin

B) As a harmless prank played on unfaithful wives

C) As a humorous but embarrassing public spectacle

D) As a long-standing tradition that she did not condone

Answer: C

Q13: What did Jopp say was the motivation behind his plan to reveal Lucetta's letters?

A) To get revenge on her for rejecting his request

B) To expose her as a dishonorable woman

C) To force her to help him find a job

D) To prevent her from harming her husband

Answer: B

Q14: What did Lucetta do with the letters after Jopp returned them to her?

A) She kept them hidden, fearing her husband might find them

B) She burned them to destroy any evidence of her past

C) She confronted Henchard and demanded he retrieve them

D) She gave them to her husband, hoping for his forgiveness

Answer: B

Q15: What did Charl and Joe do to assist the stranger in crossing the stream?

A) They rowed him across in a boat

B) They threw a plank across the water for him to walk over

C) They carried him on their backs through the shallow part

D) They told him to find another way around, as the stream was too deep

Answer: B

Q16: What did the stranger say when he encountered the stream blocking his path?

A) He refused to cross and turned back the way he came

B) He asked the men to find him another route into town

C) He declared he would simply wade through the water

D) He stated he would jump across the stream without assistance

Answer: C

Q17: Where was the actual main entrance to the inn called "Peter's Finger"?

A) Through the front door

B) At the back of the building

C) Down a narrow alley

D) Around the side of the inn

Answer: C

Q18: What did the landlady say she had been unjustly accused and jailed for in the past?

A) Theft

B) Assault

C) Arson

D) Being an accessory to a crime

Answer: D

Q19: What did the deposed gamekeeper describe to Jopp about his past altercation with another man?

A) How he had violently attacked the other man

B) How his wife had mistakenly identified him as the offender

C) How they had fought and rolled down a hill together

D) How the other man had framed him for poaching

Answer: C

Q20: What did the landlady say the stranger should not expect to find at her "respectable" inn?

A) Comfortable accommodations

B) Good food and drink

C) Entertainment and revelry

D) Any involvement in the planned "skimmity-ride"

Answer: D

Q21: Why did Jopp say he wanted to reveal the contents of Lucetta's love letters?

A) To punish her for rejecting his request for help

B) To embarrass her and ruin her standing in the community

C) To prove Henchard's involvement in her past

D) To expose the hypocrisy of her current situation

Answer: B

Q22: What did the stranger say he had heard about the "skimmity-ride" custom before?

A) That it was an ancient pagan ritual

B) That it was a common occurrence in the town

C) That it was a harmless form of community entertainment

D) That it was a way to publicly shame unfaithful wives

Answer: D

Q23: How did the landlady initially describe the "skimmity-ride" to the stranger?

A) As a lighthearted festival

B) As a private family affair

C) As an old-fashioned form of justice

D) As an "old foolish thing" done to unfaithful wives

Answer: D

Q24: What did Jopp say was the motivation behind his plan to reveal Lucetta's love letters?

A) To get back at her for rejecting his request

B) To expose her as a dishonorable woman

C) To force her to help him find a new job

D) To prevent her from further wrongdoing

Answer: B

Q25: How did the deposed gamekeeper describe the outcome of his past altercation with another man?

A) He was able to prove his innocence and avoid punishment

B) His wife's testimony led to him being wrongfully convicted

C) They were both punished for their part in the incident

D) He was able to escape without facing any legal consequences

Answer: B

Q26: What did the landlady say was the reason she did not encourage the "skimmity-ride" custom?

A) It was illegal and could lead to serious trouble

B) It went against her moral and religious beliefs

C) It would damage the respectable reputation of her inn

D) It was a waste of time and resources for the community

Answer: C

Q27: What did the stranger say he would be in Casterbridge for the next few weeks?

A) Attending a business conference

B) Visiting family members

C) Investigating criminal activities

D) Simply passing through the town

Answer: D

Q28: What did Jopp say he would do now that he had obtained Lucetta's love letters?

A) Destroy them to protect her reputation

B) Present them to her husband as evidence

C) Publish them in the local newspaper

D) Use them to initiate the planned "skimmity-ride"

Answer: D

Q29: How did the landlady describe the stranger's offer to contribute to the "skimmity-ride"?

A) As an inappropriate and unethical proposal

B) As a generous and well-meaning gesture

C) As a suspicious attempt to cause trouble

D) As a lighthearted and harmless offer

Answer: D

Q30: What did Lucetta do after receiving the letters back from Jopp?

A) She confronted Henchard about the situation

B) She pleaded with Jopp not to reveal the contents

C) She burned the letters to destroy any evidence

D) She confessed everything to her husband

Answer: C

   D. To prevent her from further wrongdoing
   **उत्तर:** B

   **उत्तर:** C

   **उत्तर:** D

# Set: 38

Q1: What event was about to take place in Casterbridge?

A) The crowning of a new king

B) A royal wedding

C) A royal visit and inauguration of an engineering work

D) The opening of a new hospital

Answer: C

Q2: How long was the Royal Personage going to halt in Casterbridge?

A) 1 hour

B) 2 hours

C) Half an hour or so

D) The whole day

Answer: C

Q3: What was the last time Royalty had been seen in Casterbridge?

A) During the reign of King George II

B) During the reign of King George III

C) During the reign of Queen Victoria

D) Never before

Answer: B

Q4: What was the address prepared on?

A) Paper

B) Parchment

C) Cloth

D) Stone

Answer: B

Q5: Who was the artist who did the ornamental lettering on the address?

A) An unnamed local artist

B) A famous calligrapher from London

C) The town sign-painter

D) The text does not specify

Answer: D

Q6: When did the Council meet to arrange the details of the royal reception?

A) The day before the event

B) The week before the event

C) The Tuesday before the event

D) The month before the event

Answer: C

Q7: Who entered the Council Chamber while they were meeting?

A) The Mayor, Farfrae

B) The town crier

C) A local dignitary

D) Michael Henchard

Answer: D

Q8: What was Henchard's request regarding the royal reception?

A) To be included in the official procession

B) To be given a special seat to view the proceedings

C) To be allowed to greet the Royal Personage

D) All of the above

Answer: D

Q9: What was the Council's response to Henchard's request?

A) They welcomed his participation

B) They were hesitant and saw it as an irregularity

C) They ignored his request

D) They offered him a minor role

Answer: B

Q10: How did Henchard respond to the Council's refusal?

A) He accepted it gracefully

B) He threatened the Council

C) He said he would welcome the Royal Personage himself

D) He stormed out of the meeting

Answer: C

Q11: What did Henchard do to prepare for the royal reception?

A) He bought a new suit of clothes

B) He decorated the streets

C) He primed himself with a glass of rum

D) He organized a parade

Answer: C

Q12: What did Henchard carry with him when he went to the royal reception?

A) A bouquet of flowers

B) A ceremonial mace

C) A Union Jack flag

D) A scroll with an address

Answer: C

Q13: Where was Elizabeth-Jane positioned to watch the proceedings?

A) In the crowd near Henchard

B) In the ladies' gallery

C) At home, unable to attend

D) With the official party

Answer: B

Q14: How did Lucetta react when she saw Henchard approach the royal carriage?

A) She greeted him warmly

B) She pretended not to notice him

C) She fainted

D) She expressed annoyance

Answer: C

Q15: What did Farfrae do when Henchard approached the royal carriage?

A) He welcomed Henchard

B) He ignored the incident

C) He dragged Henchard away

D) He called the authorities

Answer: C

Q16: What was Lucetta's relationship with Henchard described as?

A) Patron and protégé

B) Employer and employee

C) Friends

D) Strangers

Answer: B

Q17: How did Lucetta respond when Mrs. Blowbody referred to Henchard as Farfrae's "patron"?

A) She agreed with the statement

B) She corrected the statement

C) She ignored the comment

D) She expressed anger

Answer: B

Q18: What was the attitude of the crowd towards Farfrae compared to Henchard?

A) They favored Henchard over Farfrae

B) They favored Farfrae over Henchard

C) They were indifferent towards both

D) They were hostile towards both

Answer: B

Q19: What was the rumor circulating about Lucetta and Henchard in Mixen Lane?

A) They were plotting against the mayor

B) They were having an affair

C) They were planning a skimmington ride

D) They were engaged in a business deal

Answer: C

Q20: What did Longways and the others plan to do about the rumored skimmington ride?

A) Participate in the ride

B) Inform the authorities

C) Warn those involved

D) Ignore the rumor

Answer: C

Q21: What was Jopp's role in the skimmington ride plot?

A) He was against the idea

B) He was organizing the ride

C) He was merely a bystander

D) He was trying to stop it

Answer: B

Q22: How did the townspeople view Farfrae's rise in status compared to his earlier days?

A) They were still as enthusiastic about him

B) They were indifferent to his rise

C) They were somewhat less charmed by him

D) They were openly hostile towards him

Answer: C

Q23: What was the reason given for the townspeople's change in attitude towards Farfrae?

A) He had become too wealthy and powerful

B) He had married Lucetta, who was seen as undesirable

C) He had become too focused on his official duties

D) All of the above

Answer: D

Q24: How did Nance Mockridge feel about Lucetta's higher social status?

A) She was indifferent

B) She admired Lucetta

C) She wished to see Lucetta "toppered"

D) She wanted to help Lucetta

Answer: C

Q25: What was the general sentiment of the lower class townspeople towards the royal visit?

A) They were excited and enthusiastic

B) They were indifferent and uninterested

C) They were resentful and hostile

D) They were divided in their opinions

Answer: A

Q26: What was the significance of the "skimmity-ride" being mentioned in the text?

A) It was a traditional town festival

B) It was a plot to humiliate Lucetta and Farfrae

C) It was a celebration for the royal visit

D) It was a local political protest

Answer: B

Q27: How did the Council members react when Henchard entered their meeting?

A) They welcomed him warmly

B) They ignored his presence

C) They exchanged embarrassed glances

D) They asked him to leave immediately

Answer: C

Q28: What was Henchard's stated "particular reason" for wanting to participate in the royal reception?

A) He wanted to show his loyalty to the Crown

B) He wanted to be part of an important historical event

C) He wanted to spite Farfrae and the Council

D) The text does not specify his reason

Answer: D

Q29: How did Henchard's appearance and attire differ from the rest of the town during the royal visit?

A) He was more formally dressed

B) He was in his best Sunday clothes

C) He was in shabby, threadbare clothing

D) He wore a unique costume

Answer: C

Q30: What was the overall tone or mood conveyed in the passage regarding the royal visit and the tensions in Casterbridge?

A) Celebratory and harmonious

B) Tense and conflict-ridden

C) Indifferent and apathetic

D) A mix of excitement and resentment

Answer: D

## Set: 39

# Set: 39

Q1: How did the royal reception affect Lucetta emotionally?

A) She was indifferent to the event.

B) She was disappointed by the brief proceedings.

C) She was overwhelmed with excitement and triumph.

D) She was nervous and anxious throughout.

Answer: C

Q2: What did Lucetta overhear about the possibility of Farfrae receiving a knighthood?

A) It was a definite plan.

B) It was an idle rumor.

C) It was a secret plan that was not meant to be heard.

D) The text does not mention any such rumor.

Answer: B

Q3: How did Henchard react when he overheard Lucetta deny his role in helping Farfrae?

A) He was amused by her denial.

B) He felt betrayed and hurt.

C) He confronted her about it immediately.

D) He did not react, as he was indifferent to her comments.

Answer: B

Q4: What did Henchard believe Farfrae had done that he considered a "degradation"?

A) Refusing to let Henchard participate in the royal reception

B) Shaking Henchard by the collar in front of the crowd

C) Denying Henchard's help in establishing his business

D) All of the above

Answer: D

Q5: Where did Henchard go after the royal reception to confront Farfrae?

A) Farfrae's house

B) The town hall

C) The granaries

D) The local tavern

Answer: C

Q6: What did Henchard do to prepare for his confrontation with Farfrae?

A) He sharpened a weapon.

B) He armed himself with a club.

C) He tied one of his arms to his side.

D) He practiced his wrestling moves.

Answer: C

Q7: What did Henchard intend to do with Farfrae in the granary loft?

A) Throw him out the window

B) Physically assault him

C) Confront him verbally

D) Reconcile with him

Answer: A

Q8: What was Farfrae's initial reaction when Henchard confronted him in the loft?

A) He was afraid and tried to flee.

B) He was confused and asked what was wrong.

C) He became angry and tried to fight back.

D) He remained calm and tried to reason with Henchard.

Answer: B

Q9: How did Henchard describe the change in their relative positions?

A) Farfrae was no longer above him due to his wealth and status.

B) Henchard was no longer below Farfrae due to his poverty.

C) They were now equals, "man and man".

D) Both A and B

Answer: D

Q10: What was Farfrae's role as the "chief magistrate" in the confrontation?

A) He tried to arrest Henchard.

B) He had a duty to stop Henchard's disorderly behavior.

C) He attempted to mediate the situation.

D) He did not take on any official role.

Answer: B

Q11: What did Henchard propose they do to settle their dispute?

A) Have a fist fight

B) Engage in a wrestling match

C) Take the dispute to the authorities

D) Have a duel with weapons

Answer: B

Q12: What advantage did Henchard try to give himself in the wrestling match?

A) He tied one of Farfrae's arms.

B) He tied one of his own arms to his side.

C) He brought a weapon with him.

D) He chose the location by the open window.

Answer: B

Q13: What was the outcome of the physical struggle between Henchard and Farfrae?

A) Henchard was able to throw Farfrae out the window.

B) Farfrae gained the upper hand and overpowered Henchard.

C) Neither was able to gain a decisive advantage over the other.

D) Henchard had Farfrae dangling out the window, but could not bring himself to push him.

Answer: D

Q14: What emotion did the text say came over Henchard after he had Farfrae in a vulnerable position?

A) Triumph

B) Rage

C) Remorse

D) Fear

Answer: C

Q15: What did Henchard say about how he had once felt about Farfrae?

A) That he had hated him

B) That he had been jealous of him

C) That he had once loved him as a brother

D) That he had always respected him

Answer: C

Q16: After their confrontation, what did Henchard hear about Farfrae's planned movements?

A) He was leaving town immediately.

B) He was going to the local tavern.

C) He was unexpectedly called to Weatherbury.

D) He was going to report Henchard to the authorities.

Answer: C

Answer: C

Answer: B

Answer: B

Answer: C

Answer: C

Answer: B

Q23: What did Henchard mean when he said Farfrae's "money and fine wife no longer lift 'ee above me"?

C) Henchard believed Farfrae had become arrogant due to his success.

Answer: D

Answer: B

Answer: C

# Set: 40

Q1: What was the reason Farfrae decided to go to Weatherbury that evening?

A) He had a call to make at Mellstock

B) He received an anonymous letter requesting him to go

C) He wanted to recover himself before meeting Lucetta

D) He had a business matter to attend to there

Answer: B

Q2: What did the maid-servants see happening in the street?

A) A procession with two figures tied back-to-back on a donkey

B) A group of people carrying torches and musical instruments

C) Two people dancing in the street

D) A carriage passing by

Answer: A

Q3: How did Lucetta react when she saw the effigy in the street?

A) She thought it was amusing and laughed

B) She became distressed and had an epileptic seizure

C) She called the police to stop the procession

D) She went outside to get a better look

Answer: B

Q4: What did Elizabeth-Jane try to do when Lucetta saw the effigy?

A) She tried to stop Lucetta from going outside

B) She tried to close the window to block the view

C) She ran out to confront the people in the procession

D) Both A and B

Answer: D

Q5: Who was the intended target of the satirical mummery?

A) Henchard

B) Farfrae

C) Lucetta

D) Elizabeth-Jane

Answer: C

Q6: What did the constables do when they were unable to find the procession?

A) They went home, unable to do anything

B) They arrested the landlady at the Peter's Finger inn

C) They decided to wait until the next day to continue their investigation

D) They went to Mixen Lane to search for clues

Answer: D

A) Keeping time for dancing

B) Signaling the start of the procession

C) Storing it to keep it dry

D) Entertaining the patrons

Answer: C

Q8: What did Farfrae's men do to try to get him out of the way for the evening?

C) They created a disturbance to distract him

D) They told him there was a business matter he needed to attend to

Answer: A

Q1: Why did Henchard go looking for Farfrae?

A) To apologize for their earlier confrontation

B) To invite Farfrae to a celebration

C) To inform Farfrae that his wife was ill

D) To warn Farfrae about a threat to his safety

Answer: C

Q2: What did Henchard try to do to prevent Farfrae from going to Mellstock?

A) He tried to persuade Farfrae to go straight home

B) He tried to create a distraction to delay Farfrae

C) He tried to take control of Farfrae's horse and gig

D) He tried to physically block Farfrae's path

Answer: A

Q3: What did Henchard believe Farfrae might think about his motives for stopping him?

A) That Henchard was trying to rob him

B) That Henchard was trying to attack him

C) That Henchard was trying to deceive him

D) That Henchard was trying to arrest him

Answer: C

Q4: What was the reason Farfrae did not believe Henchard's claims about Lucetta being ill?

A) Farfrae trusted Lucetta completely and doubted Henchard

B) Farfrae thought Henchard was trying to lure him into a trap

C) Farfrae did not think Lucetta could become ill so quickly

D) Both B and C

Answer: D

Q5: What did the people at Farfrae's house think when Henchard returned without Farfrae?

A) They were relieved that Henchard had succeeded in finding Farfrae

B) They were disappointed that Henchard had not been able to bring Farfrae back

C) They were angry that Henchard had failed in his mission

D) They were suspicious of Henchard's motives

Answer: B

Q6: What did Henchard do after returning to his own home and learning about the visitor who had come looking for him?

A) He went out to try to find the visitor

B) He ignored the message and went to bed

C) He asked Jopp for more details about the visitor

D) He assumed the visitor was there to bring him bad news

Answer: B

Q7: How did Henchard feel about Elizabeth-Jane after observing her reaction to him at Farfrae's house?

A) He felt angry and resentful towards her

B) He felt indifferent and uninterested in her

C) He felt a sense of affection and desire to connect with her

D) He felt suspicious and distrustful of her

Answer: C

Q8: What did the messenger sent to fetch Farfrae find when he arrived back at Farfrae's house?

A) Farfrae had already returned home on his own

B) Lucetta's illness had worsened significantly

C) The doctor had already arrived to attend to Lucetta

D) Henchard was still waiting anxiously for Farfrae's return

Answer: C

Q9: What did the people in Casterbridge know about the cause of Lucetta's illness?

A) They knew the full details of the skimmington ride

B) They had suspicions about the reason but did not discuss it

C) They believed it was due to natural causes unrelated to the skimmington

D) They assumed it was caused by Henchard's confrontation with Farfrae

Answer: B

D) It is not specified what exactly she told Farfrae

Answer: D

   C. He was trying to gather information to use against Farfrae
   D. Both A and B
   **उत्तर:** D

   A. That Lucetta had died

   C. That Lucetta was refusing to see anyone who came to the door
   D. That Lucetta was deaf and could no longer hear the knocker
   **उत्तर:** A

   D. He tried to take control of Farfrae's horse and gig

## Set: 43

# Set: 43

Q1: What was the primary reason Newson left Susan and Elizabeth-Jane?

A) He felt Susan was not happy with him

B) He thought Elizabeth-Jane would have a better life with Henchard

C) He believed they would be better off without him

D) He was presumed dead after a storm at sea

Answer: D

Q2: What did Henchard say to Newson about Elizabeth-Jane's death?

A) That she had died recently

B) That she had died a year ago

C) That she was still alive

D) That he did not know when she died

Answer: B

Q3: What was Henchard's initial reaction when Newson revealed his identity?

A) Indifference

B) Hostility

C) Guilt

D) Shock

Answer: D

Q4: What did Henchard do after Newson left, thinking he had lost Elizabeth-Jane?

A) He went to the tavern

B) He went to the church

C) He went to the river

D) He went home

Answer: C

Q5: Why did Henchard lie to Newson about Elizabeth-Jane's death?

A) To keep her for himself

B) To avoid being questioned

C) To spare Newson's feelings

D) To protect Elizabeth-Jane

Answer: A

Q6: What was Elizabeth-Jane's initial reaction when Henchard told her about the "miracle" he saw in the river?

A) She believed him

B) She was skeptical

C) She was frightened

D) She was confused

Answer: B

Q7: How did Elizabeth-Jane describe Henchard's mood when she returned to see him?

A) Happy and cheerful

B) Sad and lonely

C) Angry and frustrated

D) Tired and worn out

Answer: B

Q8: What was the "effigy" that Elizabeth-Jane saw floating in the river?

A) A bundle of old clothes

B) Henchard's body

C) Lucetta's body

D) An image of Henchard

Answer: D

Q9: Where did Elizabeth-Jane think the effigy had come from?

A) It was thrown into the river by the magistrates

B) It was thrown into the river by Henchard

C) It was thrown into the river by Lucetta's enemies

D) It was thrown into the river by people trying to get rid of it

Answer: D

Q10: What did Henchard say the effigy "kept him alive"?

A) It was a reminder of his past sins

B) It meant he was still living

C) It represented his will to go on

D) It was a sign of divine intervention

Answer: D

Q11: How did Henchard's appearance change after Elizabeth-Jane agreed to live with him again?

A) He became more disheveled

B) He became more unkempt

C) He became more well-groomed

D) He became more anxious

Answer: C

Q12: What event had recently occurred that Elizabeth-Jane mentioned to Henchard?

A) The death of his wife Susan

B) The death of Elizabeth-Jane's mother

C) The death of Lucetta

D) The death of the mayor

Answer: C

Q13: How did Henchard feel about the prospect of losing Elizabeth-Jane again?

A) He was indifferent

B) He was relieved

C) He was anxious

D) He was devastated

Answer: D

Q14: Where did Newson say he had been all these years?

A) He had been in America

B) He had been at sea

C) He had been living in Falmouth

D) He had been traveling the world

Answer: B

A) He thought Susan was happier with Henchard

B) He thought Elizabeth-Jane was better off with Henchard

C) He thought Elizabeth-Jane had died

D) He was presumed lost at sea

Answer: C

Q16: What did Newson say he had planned to do after learning of Susan's death?

A) Return to Casterbridge

B) Go back to America

C) Live out his days at sea

D) Abandon Elizabeth-Jane

Answer: A

Q17: How did Henchard react when Newson mentioned the "transaction" between them years ago?

A) He denied it ever happened

B) He admitted his guilt

C) He tried to justify his actions

D) He refused to discuss it

Answer: A

A) She was too intelligent to have been deceived

B) She was too simple-minded to have understood

C) She was too trusting to have questioned it

D) She was too ashamed to have acknowledged it

Answer: B

A) To avoid further questions

B) To protect Elizabeth-Jane

C) To keep her for himself

D) To spare Newson's feelings

Answer: C

B) He was amused

C) He was terrified

D) He was relieved

Answer: C

D) She refused to discuss it

Answer: B

C) Lucetta's discarded image

D) Evidence of a crime

Answer: D

D) He felt regretful and remorseful

Answer: B

Answer: A

D) He was indifferent

Answer: C

Answer: B

   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

13. How did Henchard feel about the prospect of losing Elizabeth-Jane again?
   A. He was indifferent
   B. He was relieved
   C. He was anxious
   D. He was devastated
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

14. Where did Newson say he had been all these years?
   A. He had been in America
   B. He had been at sea
   C. He had been living in Falmouth
   D. He had been traveling the world
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

15. What was the reason Newson gave for not returning to claim Elizabeth-Jane earlier?
   A. He thought Susan was happier with Henchard
   B. He thought Elizabeth-Jane was better off with Henchard
   C. He thought Elizabeth-Jane had died
   D. He was presumed lost at sea
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

16. What did Newson say he had planned to do after learning of Susan's death?
   A. Return to Casterbridge
   B. Go back to America
   C. Live out his days at sea
   D. Abandon Elizabeth-Jane
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

17. How did Henchard react when Newson mentioned the "transaction" between them years ago?
   A. He denied it ever happened
   B. He admitted his guilt
   C. He tried to justify his actions
   D. He refused to discuss it
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

18. What did Henchard say about Susan's ability to understand the nature of the "transaction" between them?
   A. She was too intelligent to have been deceived
   B. She was too simple-minded to have understood
   C. She was too trusting to have questioned it
   D. She was too ashamed to have acknowledged it
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

19. Why did Henchard initially lie to Newson about Elizabeth-Jane's death?
   A. To avoid further questions
   B. To protect Elizabeth-Jane
   C. To keep her for himself
   D. To spare Newson's feelings
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

20. What was Henchard's reaction when he first saw Newson's "double" floating in the river?
   A. He was fascinated
   B. He was amused
   C. He was terrified
   D. He was relieved
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

21. How did Elizabeth-Jane react when Henchard asked her about miracles?
   A. She believed they were still possible
   B. She was skeptical they still occurred
   C. She was frightened by the idea
   D. She refused to discuss it
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

22. What did Elizabeth-Jane think the "effigy" in the river represented?
   A. Henchard's guilty conscience
   B. A warning from the supernatural
   C. Lucetta's discarded image
   D. Evidence of a crime
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

23. How did Henchard describe his own state of mind and lack of purpose after losing Elizabeth-Jane?
   A. He felt angry and resentful
   B. He felt lonely and despairing
   C. He felt confused and directionless
   D. He felt regretful and remorseful
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

24. What did Henchard say had "killed" Lucetta, but "kept him alive"?
   A. The effigy in the river
   B. His own deceptions
   C. Elizabeth-Jane's departure
   D. Newson's return
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

25. How did Henchard react when Elizabeth-Jane agreed to come live with him again?
   A. He was overjoyed
   B. He was skeptical
   C. He was cautious
   D. He was indifferent
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

26. What did Elizabeth-Jane notice about Henchard's appearance when she returned to see him?
   A. He was disheveled and unkempt
   B. He was clean-shaven and well-groomed
   C. He was pale and haggard
   D. He was dressed in his finest clothes
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

27. Where did Henchard take Elizabeth-Jane to show her the "miracle" he had seen?
   A. The church
   B. The market square
   C. The river at Ten Hatches
   D. His house
   **उत्तर:** C
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

28. What did Elizabeth-Jane initially think the object floating in the river was?
   A. A bundle of old clothes
   B. Henchard's body
   C. Lucetta's body
   D. The effigy of Henchard
   **उत्तर:** A
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

29. How did Henchard describe his own state of mind, saying he was "not a read man"?
   A. He was uneducated
   B. He was illiterate
   C. He was ignorant
   D. All of the above
   **उत्तर:** D
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

30. What did Elizabeth-Jane say she would do to help Henchard, despite his past roughness?
   A. She would leave him alone
   B. She would move in with him
   C. She would visit him occasionally
   D. She would find him a job
   **उत्तर:** B
   **व्याख्या:** उत्तर स्रोत पुस्तक की उत्तर-कुंजी के अनुसार है।

## Set: 44

# Set: 44

Q1: What impact did Lucetta's death have on Farfrae's plans for vengeance?

A) He immediately sought to punish those responsible

B) He decided it was not worth pursuing legal action

C) He was unsure of how to proceed

D) He resolved to wait until after the funeral

Answer: D

Q2: What business opportunity did the town council, led by Farfrae, provide for Henchard?

A) A position as mayor

B) A seed and grain shop

C) A construction contract

D) A managerial role in Farfrae's business

Answer: B

Q3: Why did Henchard accept the business opportunity offered by Farfrae?

A) He needed the money

B) He wanted to spite Farfrae

C) It was the best option available

D) He did it for Elizabeth-Jane's sake

Answer: D

Q4: What did Henchard observe about Farfrae's attitude towards Lucetta after her death?

A) He remained vengeful and bitter

B) He was indifferent and moved on quickly

C) He was nostalgic but forgiving

D) He was still deeply grieved

Answer: C

Q5: What did Henchard suspect about the reason for Elizabeth-Jane's frequent walks towards Budmouth?

A) She was meeting a secret lover

B) She was seeking solitude

C) She was visiting Newson

D) She was meeting with Farfrae

Answer: D

Q6: How did Henchard feel about the possibility of Elizabeth-Jane marrying Farfrae?

A) He actively encouraged it

B) He was indifferent to the idea

C) He was opposed to the idea

D) He was unsure about it

Answer: C

Q7: What did Henchard hope might happen if Farfrae learned that Elizabeth-Jane was not his daughter?

A) That Farfrae would abandon Elizabeth-Jane

B) That Farfrae would become more sympathetic to him

C) That Farfrae would not want to marry Elizabeth-Jane

D) That Farfrae would keep the information secret

Answer: A

Q8: What did Henchard notice about the books in Elizabeth-Jane's room?

A) They were old and worn

B) They were few in number

C) There were many more than he expected

D) They were all on the same subject

Answer: C

Q9: How did Henchard feel about Elizabeth-Jane's newfound independence?

A) He was proud of her

B) He was indifferent to it

C) He was threatened by it

D) He was overwhelmed by it

Answer: C

Q10: What did Henchard do when he saw Farfrae and Elizabeth-Jane meet on the street?

A) He confronted them angrily

B) He hid and observed them

C) He walked past them without acknowledging them

D) He turned and went the other way

Answer: B

Q11: What was the tone of the conversation between Farfrae and Elizabeth-Jane when Henchard observed them?

A) Flirtatious and romantic

B) Formal and distant

C) Friendly and casual

D) Tense and strained

Answer: C

Q12: What did Farfrae call Elizabeth-Jane when Henchard overheard them?

A) Darling

B) My dear

C) Dearest Elizabeth-Jane

D) Sweetheart

Answer: C

Q13: What was Henchard's main concern about Elizabeth-Jane marrying Farfrae?

A) That he would lose her affection

B) That Farfrae would discover his deception

C) That Farfrae would not be a good husband

D) That Elizabeth-Jane would be unhappy

Answer: A

Q14: How did Henchard describe his "solicitus timor" or anxious fear regarding Elizabeth-Jane?

A) It made him act stealthily

B) It made him more direct and confrontational

C) It made him more accepting of her choices

D) It made him more suspicious of Farfrae

Answer: A

Q15: What was Henchard's reaction when he considered telling Farfrae that Elizabeth-Jane was not his daughter?

A) He felt it would be the right thing to do

B) He felt it would correct an injustice

C) He felt it would somehow benefit Elizabeth-Jane

D) He felt it was a temptation from the "devil"

Answer: D

Q16: What drew Henchard to return to the market-place, the site of his former triumphs?

A) He wanted to relive his past success

B) He had a curious feeling to go there

C) He was looking for Farfrae

D) He was hoping to see Elizabeth-Jane

Answer: B

Q17: What did Henchard observe about the frequency of Farfrae and Elizabeth-Jane's chance meetings?

A) They were rare and accidental

B) They were regular and purposeful

C) They were increasing in frequency

D) They were decreasing over time

Answer: B

Q18: How did Henchard describe his future relationship with Farfrae if Elizabeth-Jane married him?

A) They would become close friends

B) They would have a civil but distant relationship

C) Farfrae would completely reject and ignore him

D) Henchard would have no place in their lives

Answer: D

Q19: What did Henchard notice about the quality of the new books in Elizabeth-Jane's room?

A) They were cheap and poorly made

B) They were old and well-worn

C) They were numerous and of good quality

D) They were mostly about one subject

Answer: C

Q20: How did Elizabeth-Jane describe her reason for walking towards Budmouth?

A) To get exercise

B) To visit the sea

C) To meet a friend

D) To clear her head

Answer: B

Q21: What did Farfrae say about secrets casting a "deep shadow" over his own life?

A) He was referring to a secret about his past

B) He was referring to a secret regarding Elizabeth-Jane

C) He was speaking metaphorically about secrets in general

D) He was hinting at a secret involving Henchard

Answer: A

Q22: What emotion did Henchard experience when he heard Farfrae call Elizabeth-Jane "Dearest"?

A) Joy

B) Pride

C) Jealousy

D) Relief

Answer: C

Q23: What did Henchard vow to do regarding Elizabeth-Jane and Farfrae's potential relationship?

A) He vowed to actively interfere and prevent it

B) He vowed to support and encourage it

C) He vowed to passively observe without intervening

D) He vowed to find a way to discredit Farfrae

Answer: C

Q24: What was Henchard's main concern about Elizabeth-Jane growing close to Farfrae?

A) That she would come to despise Henchard

B) That Farfrae would not treat her well

C) That she would become financially dependent on Farfrae

D) That she would forget about Henchard entirely

Answer: A

Q25: How did Henchard describe the "outer chamber of the brain" where unwanted thoughts entered?

A) As a place of temptation

B) As a place of darkness

C) As a place of refuge

D) As a place of anxiety

Answer: A

Q26: What idea crossed Henchard's mind about revealing Elizabeth-Jane's true parentage to Farfrae?

A) That it would bring him closer to Farfrae

B) That it would push Farfrae away from Elizabeth-Jane

C) That it would make Farfrae more sympathetic to Henchard

D) That it would resolve the tension between them

Answer: B

Q27: What did Henchard describe as a "visitation of the devil" that he struggled with?

A) His jealous thoughts about Farfrae and Elizabeth-Jane

B) His regrets over his past mistreatment of Elizabeth-Jane

C) His desire to reveal Elizabeth-Jane's true parentage

D) His temptation to interfere in Elizabeth-Jane's relationship

Answer: A

Q28: How did Henchard feel about the prospect of losing Elizabeth-Jane's affection and companionship?

A) He was resigned to it

B) He was indifferent to it

C) He was terrified by it

D) He was hopeful about it

Answer: C

Q29: What aspect of Farfrae's character did Henchard note when observing his interactions with Elizabeth-Jane?

A) His commanding presence

B) His intellectual superiority

C) His romantic tendencies

D) His empathetic nature

Answer: D

Q30: How did Henchard describe his own change in character compared to his former self?

A) He was more assertive and confrontational

B) He was more cautious and reserved

C) He was more resigned and accepting

D) He was more daring and impulsive

Answer: C

Q1: Where did Henchard go after leaving Casterbridge?

D) All of the above

D) A hidden treasure

D) Elizabeth-Jane

Answer: C

D) Eastward

Answer: D

D) A road leading back to Casterbridge

Answer: C

Q8: What prominent feature did Elizabeth-Jane and Farfrae pass on their search for Henchard?

A) A lush green valley

C) A wide river

D) A bustling market town

Answer: B

D) It started to rain heavily

Answer: B

Q10: Who did Elizabeth-Jane and Farfrae encounter near a cottage in a ravine?

A) Newson

B) Henchard

C) Abel Whittle

D) A stranger

Answer: C

A) He was ill

D) He was on vacation

Answer: B
